# The Sun of Auspicious Moments

## With the Commentary Titled "The Beloved of the Sun"

### 80 (Eighty) Folios <insert>(Astronomy/Astrology)</insert> <note>The term 'Jyotisha' refers to the traditional Indian system of time-keeping, astronomy, and astrology.</note>

<note>original: "Muhurta-Martanda" — The Sun of Auspicious Moments. 'Muhurta' refers to the selection of the right time for rituals or actions.</note>

<meta>This page continues the technical analysis of astrological conditions for victory in conflict, likely following a discussion on planetary strengths. The text is a commentary on the Muhurtamartanda, a classic text on electional astrology (Muhurta).</meta>

<page-num>82</page-num>

If the birth ascendant <note>Janma Lagna: the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth</note> and the birth moon-sign <note>Janma Rashi: the sign the moon occupied at birth</note> are present in the query ascendant <note>Prashna Lagna: the zodiac sign rising at the moment a question is asked to the astrologer</note>, it is a configuration for the King's victory; this is the first yoga <note>Yoga: a specific planetary combination or "union" that produces a particular result</note>. Or, if the lords of the birth ascendant and birth moon-sign are present in the query ascendant, then the King's victory is the second yoga. Or, if the query ascendant falls in the 3rd, 6th, 10th, or 11th positions <note>These are known as the Upachaya or "houses of growth/improvement"</note> from the birth ascendant or birth moon-sign, then the King's victory is the third yoga. Or, if the enemy's birth ascendant or birth moon-sign falls in the 4th, 7th, or 9th positions from the query ascendant, then the King's victory is the fourth yoga. Or, if the lords of the enemy's birth ascendant and birth moon-sign are in the 4th or 7th positions from the query ascendant, then the King's victory is the fifth yoga. If the King's birth ascendant and birth moon-sign are in the "growth" positions (3rd, 6th, 10th, or 11th) and the query ascendant is in the 4th <note>Hivuka: a technical term for the 4th house</note> or 7th <note>Dyuna: a technical term for the 7th house</note> positions, then the King's victory is the sixth yoga. Or, if the divisional strengths <note>Shadvarga: the six-fold division of a sign used to determine planetary strength</note> of the benefic planets are in the query ascendant, then the King's victory is the seventh yoga. Or, if the "head-rising" signs <note>Shirshodaya: signs that rise with their heads first, generally considered auspicious and powerful</note> are in the query ascendant, then the King's victory is the eighth yoga.

--- Page 5 ---
<meta>This page marks the beginning of the commentary. The text opens with a traditional invocation and a dual-layered interpretation of the title.</meta>

->|| Salutations to the Holy Lord Ganesha ||<-

<term>Mārtāṇḍa</term> <note>A name for the Sun, literally "born from a dead egg," signifying the cosmic egg of creation.</note> shall surely inspire our speech; he is the one who awakens us and destroys darkness through his steps <note>or rays</note>. With a body of supreme beauty, he is to be served, the granter of desires, and the eye of the universe.

This verse possesses a double meaning. First, regarding the **Sun**:
The Holy Sun (Mārtāṇḍa) inspires our speech. What are his characteristics? He possesses a "body of supreme beauty" <note>original: hastavara-tanuḥ</note>; he is present within the solar orb. Thus it is said in the <term>Smṛti</term> <note>Traditional legal and religious texts</note>: "He who resides in the solar orb, holding the conch and the discus, seated upon a lotus, beautiful and eternal." Another Smṛti says: "The Person who is seen within that solar orb is the Lord," and so on. Furthermore, what is his nature? Through his "steps" (rays), he "destroys the darkness of the waking world." He removes the cold or the darkness of ignorance. Furthermore, he is "to be served," meaning he is worthy of devotion through specific acts of service. He is the "granter of desires," meaning when worshipped, he bestows the sought-after goals. He is the "eye of the universe" <note>original: Viśvalocana</note> because he is the illuminator; he is the eye of the world and the lord of the planets, as stated in the <term>Ādityahṛdaya</term> <note>"The Heart of the Sun," a famous hymn from the Ramayana</note>.

Now, regarding the **Book**:
The text titled <term>Muhūrttamārtāṇḍa</term> <note>"The Sun of Auspicious Moments," a famous 16th-century astrological text</note> shall inspire our speech. What is its nature? It has a "body of supreme beauty" because its form is composed of poetic meters such as <term>Śārdūlavikrīḍita</term> <note>"The Tiger's Sport," a complex Sanskrit meter of 19 syllables</note>. Furthermore, what is its effect? Through its "steps" (the quarters of its verses), it "destroys the darkness of the waking world," meaning it removes the ignorance or foolishness of the reader. It is "to be served," meaning it should be studied constantly. It is the "granter of desires" because by performing actions at the auspicious moments (Muhūrttas) prescribed within it, one achieves success in their goals. It is the "eye of the universe" because it illuminates the nature of Time.

Thus, this commentary on the *Muhūrttamārtāṇḍa*, titled <term>Mārtāṇḍavallabhā</term> <note>"The Beloved of the Sun"</note>, is composed for the benefit of the world, so that there may be no enmity toward it. || 1 ||

In this science, the <term>Relationship, Subject Matter, and Purpose</term> <note>The "Anubandha Chatushtaya," a traditional four-fold introduction to a Sanskrit treatise</note> are now stated. The "Relationship" is that which has flowed down from the Creator Brahma and others. The sage <term>Nārada</term> said in his own collection: "The supreme Science of the Stars (<term>Jyotiḥśāstra</term>) is the pure eye of the Vedas." Therefore, the relationship here is that this work is a limb of the Vedas (<term>Vedāṅga</term>). It is also said here: "This short book was composed by taking the essence of the works of the Sun God."

The "Subject Matter" <note>original: Abhidheya</note> includes the stars to be avoided, the movement of planets, the sacraments such as conception, marriage, the establishment of the sacred fire, architecture, travel, friendship, days of non-study, the movement of the house-lizard <note>omens</note>, and the transitions of the sun (solstices). As Nārada says: "The subject matter is the determination of the auspicious and inauspicious for the world." Its "Purpose" is...

<summary>This opening page introduces the "Mārtāṇḍavallabhā" commentary on the astrological treatise "Muhūrttamārtāṇḍa." It uses a sophisticated double-entendre to praise both the Sun God and the text itself, establishing the book's divine authority as a branch of Vedic knowledge.</summary>
<keywords>Mārtāṇḍa, Sun God, Astrology, Muhūrtta, Sanskrit Poetics, Vedic Science, Nārada, Auspicious Moments, Jyotiḥśāstra</keywords>

--- Page 6 ---
<meta>This page marks the beginning of the commentary on the Muhurta Martanda. The author, Narayana, establishes the traditional fourfold prerequisites (Anubandha Chatushtaya) for the text: the subject matter, the relationship, the purpose, and the intended audience.</meta>

<margin>Fortune</margin> Now, one might ask: why bother with these explanations if the goal is simply to gain knowledge of a single subject? We respond that people do not engage with a scientific treatise unless the **relationship**, the **subject matter**, and the **purpose** are first explained. As it has been said: "Since the success of a listener’s engagement comes from explaining the connections, the relationship is stated first in all treatises." If the subject matter and its utility are not explained by someone, listen to the result of that: "Until the purpose of any treatise or specific action is stated, who will take it up?" 

Who is the qualified student (<term>Adhikari</term> <note>The person deemed fit to study a specific sacred or technical text</note>) for this treatise? It is the <term>Dvija</term> <note>Literally "twice-born," referring to the three upper social classes of classical India who undergo the sacred thread ceremony</note>, because this is a limb of the Vedas. Since the Veda has six limbs, it must be studied and understood by the twice-born. Thus it is said in Bhaskara’s *Crest-Jewel of Siddhantas* (<term>Siddhanta Shiromani</term> <note>A seminal 12th-century mathematical and astronomical work</note>): "Therefore, this sacred and supreme truth must be studied by the twice-born. The man who knows **astrology** (<term>Jyotisha</term>) fully attains virtue, wealth, liberation, and fame." 

One might object that starting a treatise is difficult, for "all undertakings are clouded by defects, as fire is by smoke." However, keeping in mind the purpose established by the Vedas—that "by merit, one strikes away sin"—and following the sign of the Vedic verse "May Indra of great fame grant us well-being," the author performs an auspicious invocation (<term>Mangalacharana</term>) to ensure the completion of the book without obstacles. Therefore, Narayana, a Brahmin of the Madhyandina school who has obtained the grace of the Lord Narayana, desiring to compose this section of the astrological sciences (<term>Samhita Skandha</term>), makes his vow to compose the book in the *Praharshini* meter, preceded by a salutation to his chosen deity to pacify all obstacles:

-> || Having bowed to the elephant-headed god adorned with vermilion, to the Mother, to the feet of Lord Vishnu, and to my teachers, I extend this brief and accessible *Sun of Auspicious Moments* (<term>Muhurta Martanda</term>) for the joy of the learned and for the successful accomplishment of deeds. || 1 || <-

"Adorned with vermilion" refers to the beautiful elephant-faced god (Ganesha). "The Mother" refers to Saraswati (the goddess of learning). Having bowed to the feet of Lord Vishnu and to his own teachers—the instructors of the sciences—I, Narayana, "extend" or expand upon this book named *Muhurta Martanda*. For what purpose? "For success," meaning the accomplishment of tasks. For what further purpose? "For the joy of the wise," meaning for the delight of scholars. He explains the specific qualities that cause this joy: What is this *Muhurta Martanda* like? It is "brief," meaning it is very concise, which removes the fear of it being too long to read. Furthermore, what is it? It is "rich in meaning," which removes the fear that a short book might lack necessary information. Furthermore, what is it? It is "accessible" in its methods. 

<margin>In</margin>
<margin>the</margin>
<margin>beginning</margin>

Now, he explains the names of the auspicious moments (<term>Muhurtas</term>)...

<summary>This opening page introduces the "Muhurta Martanda," a manual on electional astrology, justifying its study through Vedic authority and offering an initial prayer to Ganesha, Saraswati, and Vishnu. The commentator, Narayana, promises a work that is both concise and deep in meaning to assist practitioners in timing their actions successfully.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of inauspicious times (doshas) for beginning significant activities or rituals, following the previous page's focus on planetary alignments.</meta>

<page-num>2</page-num>

<margin>The equality of declination between the Sun and the Moon <note>original: "sūryacandrayoḥ krānti sāmyaṃ"</note></margin> <margin>At midnight and midday, ten minutes before and after must be avoided.</margin> <margin>The mother's menstrual period.</margin> <margin>From the month of birth, the birth date, birth star, and birth day of the week.</margin>

The author explains the "Kamalikka" <note>a specific set of astrological flaws</note> through three verses in the **Shardulavikridita** <term>Shardulavikridita: a complex Sanskrit poetic meter meaning "tiger's sport"</term> meter. Experts recognize the half-watch (90 minutes), the *Kulika* period, and the three days of the moon's waning. Also to be avoided are the *Parigha* yoga's first half, the *Vishti* (Bhadrā) karana, the *Vaidhriti* and *Vyatipata* yogas, the solar transition (Sankranti), the *Gandanta* <note>junction points between signs</note>, and the *Ekargala* flaw. One must also avoid the four days of the "Waning Moon" <note>original: "kusmānaṃ"</note>, and the time when the Sun and Moon reach equal declination <insert>rest</insert>. Furthermore, avoid the hours (Horas) of malefic planets, and the exact midpoints of day and night. If these occur on the anniversary of the death of one's parents, or during the mother's menses, they are particularly harmful. || 2 || Within half a month of a birth, an eclipse, or the appearance of a comet; or during the month, lunar day, or star of one's own birth if afflicted by malefic planets. One should avoid the "Steel-like" flaws in the *Gandanta* and other junctions. For those performing deeds of merit, the specific ghatikas <term>Ghatika: a unit of time equal to 24 minutes</term> must be calculated. || 3 || Here, the "Malefic Wheel" is considered inauspicious: the Moon in the tenth house of misfortune, the birth sign, or when the Moon is eclipsed or situated between two malefic planets (the "Scissor" formation). One should abandon even auspicious pursuits if there is mental distress, illness, or if the omens regarding the household are poor. || 4 || 

Regarding the question of why the "half-watch" and others should be abandoned: the *Kulika* period has specific characteristics. The "three days" refers to the 13th, 14th, and 15th lunar days. The *Vishti* karana lasts for sixty ghatikas. The "first half of *Parigha*" refers to the first 30 ghatikas of that specific astrological union. The *Sankramana* refers to the Sun or other planets moving from one zodiac sign to another; the specific minutes to be avoided during these transitions will be explained later. *Gandanta* is of three types, also to be explained. *Ekargala* will be detailed further on. The "four days of the waning moon" refers to the period from the 13th day of the dark fortnight to the 1st day of the bright fortnight. Some say this starts from the 12th day. The equality of declination between the Sun and Moon is found through calculation, yet it must be observed here. The "malefic hour" refers to the *Hora* of a cruel planet. Regarding "midday and midnight": because the transition of time resides in these moments, one must avoid ten *palas* <term>Pala: a unit of time, roughly 24 seconds</term> before and after. "Parents" refers to both the mother and father; specifically the anniversary of their death. "Mother's menses" refers to her period of impurity. "Half a month from birth" refers to the fifteen days following a birth or an eclipse.

<meta>This page continues the discussion of astrological timing and prohibitions, likely following a section on auspicious beginnings. The text focuses on "Doshas" (flaws) or inauspicious periods to avoid.</meta>

### Thus, concerning the enjoyment of that which remains in passion [or attachment] 2

<margin>Jyeshtha 2</margin>

Neither the lunar mansions <note>original: "Nakshatra"</note> nor the planets <note>original: "Graha"</note>... the wind shall be described. The "bad time" <note>original: "Duh-salu"</note> and the "ill-omened moment" <note>original: "Duh-muhurta"</note> of the first-born shall be described. Regarding the eldest child, the month of Jyeshtha <note>the lunar month falling in May/June, often considered inauspicious for the marriage of the eldest child</note>. When joined with malefic planets <note>original: "Papa-yugite"</note>... Malefic planets shall be described in general terms... thus these lunar mansions are situated poorly. Poisonous periods <note>original: "Visha"</note> shall be described along with their results. The diminished and additional months <note>original: "Kshaya-masa" and "Adhik-masa"; these are intercalary months in the lunar calendar used to keep it in sync with the solar year</note>; their results will be described. In the junctions <note>original: "Yuti"</note>, one should know the Vyatipat and Gandanta <note>specific inauspicious astrological turnings</note>.

The "striking" <note>original: "Vighata"</note> hours <note>original: "Ghatika"</note> of the instruments are as follows: Vishkumbha, Vyaghata, Shula, Vajra, Ganda, and Atiganda <note>these are names of specific astrological 'Yogas' or joinings considered difficult</note>. In these turnings, the hours to be avoided for actions are, in order: three in Vishkumbha, [one] in Vyaghata, three in Shula, five in [Ganda], [one] in Vajra, nine, six in Ganda, and [eight] in Atiganda. These are the "striking" hours. The "poisonous pulses" <note>original: "Visha-nadika"</note> of the lunar mansions will be described. The five-fold "kick" <note>original: "Latta," a planetary affliction</note> and the malefic divisions... these three will be described. An inauspicious Moon. The "obstruction" <note>original: "Vedha"</note> in the section on planetary transits <note>original: "Gochara"</note>.

The direction of the enemies is to be understood from the science of horoscopy <note>original: "Jataka-shastra"</note>. The Lord of Birth <note>the planet ruling the sign occupied at birth</note>, Jupiter <note>original: "Ijya"</note>, and Venus <note>original: "Sita"</note>—their setting <note>original: "Astamana"</note> is the disappearance of their rays by the Sun. For the body and the sign <note>Lagna and Rashi</note>, the "cruel scissors" <note>original: "Kartari," when a house is hemmed in by malefic planets</note>; the result of two cruel planets will be described. Misfortune <note>original: "Rishta"</note> arising from transits; I will explain the excess of bad planets in the transit section.

Impurity <note>original: "Sutaka"</note> arises from planetary eclipses and from birth or death. From these, impurity is produced. Among them, the impurity of an eclipse will be described. Bad dreams at the time of beginning, or hearing such words. Heartbreak <note>original: "Mano-bhanga"</note>; the non-functioning of the inner self. Anxiety <note>original: "Adhi"</note> and feverish disease. According to the sage Vagbhata <note>a famous ancient physician</note>: "Disease is the transformation of misery." One should not perform auspicious ceremonies for one suffering from fever.

And so it is said in the *Light of Astrology* <note>original: "Jyotih-prakasha"</note>:
The Lord of Birth, the Lord of the Ascendant <note>original: "Lagna"</note>, the Moon, Venus, and Jupiter; their lack of rays <note>combustion</note>, the birth month, the birth mansion, and the birth day. Ill omens, heartbreak, the diminished and additional months. Impurity from death or birth, and during an eclipse. Fever, the menstruation of a brother's wife <note>or similar familial impurity</note>, and the anniversary of the parents' death. The three-fold Gandanta, the transition of the Sun <note>original: "Sankranti"</note>, the Vyatipata and Vaidhriti <note>specific bad astrological alignments</note>. The movement of the Sun, the periods of Kulika and Tyajya. When the fortune is joined with cruel planets like Rahu, Ketu <note>original: "Shikhi"</note>, or the "Smoky" <note>original: "Dhumita"</note> point. An eclipse, a piercing <note>original: "Viddha"</note> by the Moon. The rule: when the Ascendant is joined with a cruel planet, or the Lord of the Ascendant goes to the house of enemies or death. When the Moon is poorly situated from birth, or approaches the house of death <note>8th house</note> of the Ascendant.

<summary>This page catalogs various inauspicious astrological conditions (Doshas) such as malefic planetary alignments, intercalary months, and ritual impurities that prohibit the performance of auspicious ceremonies. It cites the medical authority Vagbhata and the astrological text Jyotih-prakasha to emphasize the psychological and physical states that disqualify a person from ritual activity.</summary>

--- Page 9 ---
<meta>Folio 3; continues the discussion on astrological timing (Muhurta) from the previous folio, specifically focusing on the "Tyajya-prakarana" or the chapter on periods to be avoided for auspicious activities.</meta>

# On the Avoidance of Inauspicious Times

From the birth sign <note>Janma-rashi</note> and the birth ascendant <note>Janma-lagna</note>, as well as the current ascendant and its ninth-division <note>Navamsha</note>, one must observe the eighth house. The ascendant is considered afflicted if it falls between two "cruel" planets <note>Malefics like Mars or Saturn</note>, if the Moon is <unclear>weak/waning</unclear>, or if it falls in a bad ninth-division. Similarly, a day ruled by a cruel planet, a cruel hour <note>Hora</note>, and corrupted planetary combinations <note>Yogas</note> are to be avoided.

The specific lunar days <note>Tithis</note>, aspects, and planetary conjunctions, along with the "poisonous" minutes <note>Visha-nadika</note> of certain stars, are declared as faults <note>Doshas</note>. These are condemned for any auspicious work. Ten types of evils, including portents <note>Utpatas</note>, the transit of planets <note>Gochara</note>, and the "Latta" <note>planetary kick</note>, the "Chanda-astra" <note>fierce weapon</note>, and the "five-fold combustion" <note>Panchaka</note> are noted. These auspicious and inauspicious combinations are well-known in their respective regions.

### On Secondary Planets and Repeated Faults

Now, regarding the faults residing in secondary planets <note>Upagrahas</note>: what should be done and what should be avoided? The naming of months and other divisions is established by convention. Any repetition found here is not a mistake but for clarity. The author explains this with "not to be done" in the following:

One should not perform travels, baths, or similar rites when the Moon is in the 8th, 9th, 10th, 11th, 12th, 18th, 19th, 5th, or 25th <gloss>2nd, 2nd, 2nd, 5th</gloss> positions from the Sun's constellation. These are not beautiful <note>auspicious</note> for such deeds.

The names of the months, solstices, yogas, and lunar days must be understood. If there is repetition in some places, it is for the sake of reinforcing memory. 5. From the Sun's constellation, the 10th, 19th, and 25th are noted. Specifically, counting from the Sun's star to the Moon's star: in the 21st and others, auspicious works should not be performed. This means one should not undertake journeys or installations.

It is said: "The Moon situated in the 5th house is condemned by the experts of the spheres <note>astronomers</note>, and likewise it is said for the 19th in sequence."

In the <term>Daivajnavallabha</term> <note>A famous 10th-century astrological treatise attributed to Shripati or Varahamihira</note>, it is said: "I shall explain in context the sequence of whirlwinds, earthquakes, thunderbolts, and halos around the sun or moon, as known by the learned."

In this book, the names of months and so on are established by worldly usage; their repetition here is not a fault but for the purpose of easy recollection.
- Months: <term>Chaitra and others</term> <note>The lunar months starting with the spring month of Chaitra</note>.
- Seasons: <term>Vasanta and others</term> <note>The six Indian seasons starting with Spring</note>.
- Solstices: <term>Northern and Southern transits</term> <note>Uttarayana and Dakshinayana</note>.
- Days: <term>Sunday and others</term>.
- Yogas: <term>Vishkumbha and others</term> <note>The 27 mathematical combinations of the sun and moon</note>.

Where repetition occurs in this text, it is for the sake of firming up the memory <note>Smriti-pratyaya</note> and is not a defect.

Thus ends the chapter on things to be avoided in the <unclear>Valabha</unclear> commentary on the <term>Ratnamala of Shripati</term>. || 2 ||

|| Now, the general presiding deities of the lunar mansions are described in a single verse <note>original: "vastu raho nakshatradevatahastenaikena"</note>. In this section, the verses are composed in the *Shardulavikridita* meter <note>A complex Sanskrit poetic meter consisting of 19 syllables per line, often used for authoritative texts</note>. Starting from Aries:

The Twin Physicians, the Lord of Death, Fire, the Creator, the Moon, the Lord of the Mountains, the Mother of Gods, the Teacher of Speech, and the Serpents; then the Ancestors who end the offerings, Fortune, the Patron, the Sun, the Celestial Architect, the Wind, and the Dual Lords of Indra and Fire. Then the Friend, the King of Gods, the Demon of Destruction, and the Waters; the Universal Gods, the Creator, the Pervader, the Wealthy Ones, the Lord of Nooses, the One-Footed Goat, and thereafter the Serpent of the Deep, and finally the Nourisher.

The connection is as follows: starting from the first mansion, *Ashwini*, the lords of the mansions <term>Nakshatras: the 27 or 28 divisions of the sky used in Indian astrology</term> are:

*   **The Twin Physicians** (*Dasrau*) are the lords of *Ashwini*.
*   **The Lord of Death** (*Yama*) of *Bharani*.
*   **Fire** (*Agni*) of *Krittika*.
*   **The Creator** (*Ka* or *Brahma*) of *Rohini*.
*   **The Moon** (*Indu*) of *Mrigashira*.
*   **The Great God** (*Girisha* or *Shiva*) of *Ardra*.
*   **The Mother of Gods** (*Aditi*) of *Punarvasu*.
*   **The Teacher of Speech** (*Gira-guru* or *Brihaspati*) of *Pushya*.
*   **The Serpents** (*Pannagah*) of *Ashlesha*.
*   **The Ancestors** (*Pitarah*) of *Magha*.
*   **Fortune** (*Bhaga*) of *Purva Phalguni*.
*   **The Patron** (*Aryaman*) of *Uttara Phalguni*.
*   **The Sun** (*Ravi*) of *Hasta*.
*   **The Celestial Architect** (*Tvashtri*) of *Chitra*.
*   **The Wind** (*Anila* or *Vayu*) of *Svati*.
*   **The Dual Lords** (*Indragni*) of *Vishakha*.
*   **The Friend** (*Mitra*) of *Anuradha*.
*   **The King of Gods** (*Indra*) of *Jyeshtha*.
*   **The Demon of Destruction** (*Nirriti*) of *Mula*.
*   **The Waters** (*Jalam* or *Varuna*) of *Purva Ashadha*.
*   **The Universal Gods** (*Vishvedevas*) of *Uttara Ashadha*.
*   **The Creator** (*Ka* or *Brahma*) of *Abhijit* <note>The 28th intercalary Nakshatra</note>.
*   **The Pervader** (*Vishnu*) of *Shravana*.
*   **The Wealthy Ones** (*Vasus*) of *Dhanistha*.
*   **The Lord of Nooses** (*Pashi* or *Varuna*) of *Shatabhisha*.
*   **The One-Footed Goat** (*Ajapada*) of *Purva Bhadrapada*.
*   **The Serpent of the Deep** (*Ahirbudhnya*) of *Uttara Bhadrapada*.
*   **The Nourisher** (*Pushan*) of *Revati*.

The purpose of this is for the knowledge of the mansions and their essential nature. Now, the classifications of the mansions—such as "Light," "Fixed," etc.—and their purposes are stated in two verses:

The three "Later" mansions <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, Uttara Bhadrapada</note> along with *Rohini* are **Fixed** (*Sthira*). The "Former" mansions <note>Purva Phalguni, Purva Ashadha, Purva Bhadrapada</note> along with *Magha* and *Bharani* are **Cruel** or **Fierce** (*Krura/Ugra*). *Punarvasu*, *Svati*, and *Shravana* <insert>along with others</insert> are **Movable** (*Chara*). *Ashwini*, *Hasta*, and *Pushya* are **Light** and **Swift** (*Laghu/Kshipra*). *Chitra*, *Mrigashira*, *Anuradha*, and *Revati* are **Soft** (*Mridu*). *Vishakha* and *Krittika* are **Mixed** (*Mishra*). *Mula* and the others are **Sharp** or **Dreadful** (*Tikshna/Daruna*). This has been remembered by the ten great sages. Wise men act according to these names to ensure the success of their works.

The meaning of this is: Fixed, Movable, Light, Swift, Soft, Dreadful, and Sharp... <note>The text cuts off as it begins to further explain the astrological applications of these categories</note>

# Page 4

The malefic planets <note>original: "papa"</note> are joined with Mercury when he is weak. | 1 | **Brahma** refers to the **Rohini** constellation. What are its characteristics? It is joined with the "Three Uttras" <note>The three constellations beginning with the name Uttara: Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>. This group is known as **Dhruva** (Fixed) or **Sthira** (Stable); indeed, this nomenclature has been remembered and passed down by the Great Sages <note>original: "munindrai"</note>.

Next, regarding the **Bharani** constellation: it belongs to the group of five known as **Krura** (Cruel) or **Ugra** (Fierce), as stated in the traditional teachings.

The three stars beginning with **Shravana** <note>Shravana, Dhanishta, and Shatabhisha</note> along with **Swati** and **Punarvasu** <note>original: "rakhya"</note> constitute the group of five known as **Chara** (Movable) or **Chala** (Unsteady).

**Dasra** (Ashwini), the three **Hasta** stars, and **Abhijit** (which will be discussed later) along with the star of **Ijya** (Jupiter), which is **Pushya**—these four are declared to be **Laghu** (Light) or **Kshipra** (Swift).

**Maitra** (Anuradha), **Mri** (Mrigashira), **Indu** (the Moon's star), and **Tvashtra** (Chitra)—these four are declared to be **Mridu** (Soft/Gentle) as well as **Mitra** (Friendly).

**Agni** (Kruttika) and **Vishakha**—these two are declared to be **Sadharana** (Ordinary/Mixed) or **Mishra**. | 1 |

**Aslesha**, **Indra** (Jyeshtha), **Shiva** (Ardra), and the well-known **Mula**—these four are **Daruna** (Dreadful); these same ones are also called **Tikshna** (Sharp/Pungent). | 2 |

In these constellations, categorized as "Fixed" and so on, the wise and the learned <note>original: "panditashcha"</note> perform actions that correspond to the name of the category. For what purpose? For the sake of complete success <note>original: "samsiddhaye"</note>. This is what is meant: in a **Fixed** constellation, one should perform stable acts like the installation of a pillar <note>foundation ceremonies</note>; in a **Movable** constellation, one should perform movable acts. This is clear. Here, the use of two names for the same meaning is done to enhance the poetic quality of the composition.

Now, in this context, the author speaks to describe the benefic or malefic nature of the Sun and other planets. **The Sun and others:** The Sun is Fixed, the Moon is Movable, Mars is Fierce, Mercury is Mixed (when joined), Jupiter is Swift, Venus is Soft, and Saturn is Dreadful.

Now, regarding the Malefic and Benefic planets: This is stated in the science of Nativity <note>original: "Jataka"</note>. When the Moon is waning, and Mars is in Cancer <note>unclear context, likely referring to specific debilities</note>, they become malefic. Benefics also become so when joined with them. Rahu and Ketu are the most malefic. Mercury becomes malefic when joined with a malefic. To understand this for the sake of achieving goals:

**Arka** is the Sun. **Yama** is Saturn. **Ara** is Mars. **Rahu** is well-known. **Shikhi** is Ketu. These are the "Sharp" and malefic ones. Mercury is malefic when joined with these malefics. Others are **Saumya** (Benefic). On a "Cruel" day <note>days ruled by malefic planets</note>, one should perform cruel deeds; during "Gentle" influences, one should perform auspicious deeds.

Now, in this context, the author describes the rulers of the day, night, and the Muhurtas <note>traditional units of time, approx. 48 minutes</note> which correspond to the constellations in a single verse: Arka, Su, Shiva, Apya, Mitra, Pitara, Vasu, Ambu, Vishve, Abhijit...

# The Divisions of Time and the Rites of Conception

<note>The following section lists the fifteen Muhurtas (units of time approximately 48 minutes long) for both day and night, each governed by a specific deity.</note>

The day is divided into fifteen parts, and the night likewise. The deities of the day are: 
1. **Shiva** <note>The Auspicious One</note>
2. **Sarpa** <note>The Serpent</note>
3. **Mitra** <note>The Friend/Sun</note>
4. **Pitara** <note>The Ancestors</note>
5. **Vasu** <note>The Elemental Gods</note>
6. **Ambu** <note>The Waters</note>
7. **Vishve** <note>The Universal Gods</note>
8. **Vijita** <note>The Victorious</note>
9. **Brahma** <note>The Creator</note>
10. **Indra** <note>The King of Gods</note>
11. **Krshanu** <note>The Fire</note>
12. **Nirriti** <note>The Lord of Destruction/Kopapati</note>
13. **Varuna** <note>The Lord of Oceans</note>
14. **Yama** <note>The Lord of Death</note>
15. **Bhaga** <note>The Dispenser of Fortune</note>

Now, for the night, the first is **Shiva** (also called Smaraha). Then follow the three steps of the Goat <note>referring to the constellations Aja-Ekapada and Ahirbudhnya</note>. In order: 
2. **Aja-Charana** 
3. **Ahirbudhnya** 
4. **Pushan** 
5. **Dasra** <note>The Ashvins/Twin Physicians</note>
6. **Yama** 
7. **Agni** <note>Fire</note>
8. **Brahma** 
9. **Indra** 
10. **Aditi** <note>Mother of the Gods</note>
11. **Jiva** <note>Jupiter/The Teacher</note>
12. **Vishnu** <note>The Preserver</note>
13. **Ravi** <note>The Sun</note>
14. **Tvashtar** <note>The Celestial Architect</note>
15. **Anila** <note>The Wind</note>

The purpose is stated: "The work is remembered through its enjoyment." In these <term>Muhurtas</term> <note>specific time windows</note>, the actions prescribed for the corresponding <term>Nakshatras</term> <note>lunar mansions/stars</note> should be performed. This is the opinion of the great sages. This means: in the Muhurta of Shiva, one should perform actions prescribed for the *Ardra* star. In the Muhurta of the Serpent, perform actions for *Ashlesha*. In the Muhurta of Mitra, perform actions for *Anuradha*. 

As stated in the *Ratnamala* <note>The "Jewel Garland," a famous astrological text</note>: "Whatever action is prescribed for a specific deity, that same action should be performed during the Muhurta belonging to that deity." Just as a servant knows the desires of the Lord of the World, the names of the deities are used interchangeably with the stars and lunar days. For example, "Naga" refers to the fifth lunar day, "Dik" to the tenth, and "Bhuteshvara" to the thirteenth.

**Thus ends the section on the Stars in the *Martanda Vallabha* commentary on the *Muhurta Martanda*.**

At the first menses, one should avoid the months of Pausha, Pushka <note>Kartika</note>, and Magha. Avoid Tuesdays and Saturdays, and the "Empty" lunar days <note>Rikta Tithis: 4th, 9th, and 14th</note>. The following positions are considered <unclear>unfavorable</unclear>: 4, 8, 12...

The evening twilight is not recommended. <note>The text discusses the 'Ritu' or the first onset of menses, which in Vedic astrology is used to predict a woman's future character and fortune.</note> Friends and family... <unclear>withholding</unclear>... at night... the sin of that moment... along with...

...making the woman weak <unclear>performing</unclear>. Regarding this, the results of the months are stated: The first onset in the months of Pausha, Shukra (Jyeshtha), Urja (Kartika), Madhu (Chaitra), Shuchi (Ashadha), and Nabhas (Bhadrapada) are not auspicious. This is the sequence.

<margin>5 S</margin> The result of devotion. Pausha is well-known <note>December/January</note>. Shukra is Jyeshtha <note>May/June</note>. Urja is Kartika <note>October/November</note>. Madhu is Chaitra <note>March/April</note>. Shuchi is Ashadha <note>June/July</note>. These six months are not auspicious.

By implication, the others are auspicious. This is stated in the *Jyotir-nibandha* <note>A classical compendium of light/astrology</note>: If a woman has her first menses in Magha, she certainly becomes a widow. In Vaishakha, she is rich in wealth and sons. In Jyeshtha, she will be afflicted by disease. In Ashadha, she dies before her husband. In Shravana, she is endowed with wealth. In Bhadrapada, she will be unfortunate. In Ashvina, she will be an ascetic <note>or one who practices penance</note>. In Urja (Kartika), the woman is long-lived. In Margashirsha, she has many children. In Pausha, she is a woman of loose character. In Magha, she is endowed with the happiness of sons. In Phalguna, she is a glorious and virtuous wife. Thus are the results of the months remembered.

Now the results of the *Yogas* <note>astrological alignments of the sun and moon</note> are stated. Regarding "Bad Yogas": Among the Yogas, nine are considered wicked. These are Vishkumbha, Atiganda, Ganda, Vyaghata, Vajra, Vyatipata, Parigha, and Vaidhriti. These Yogas are not auspicious. By implication, the others are auspicious. A woman who begins her first menses in Vishkumbha becomes unfortunate. In Atiganda, the result is widowhood. In Ganda, she becomes a woman of loose character. In Vyaghata, she becomes suicidal <note>literally "self-striking"</note>. In Vajra, she is said to be unchaste. In Vyatipata, she is a destroyer. In Parigha, she is a "dead-barren" woman <note>one whose children do not survive</note>. In Vaidhriti, she kills her husband. The remaining Yogas bring auspicious results according to the meaning of their names.

Now the results of the *Varas* <note>days of the week</note> are stated. Regarding "Sinful Days": The sharp days of the Sun (Sunday), Saturn (Saturday), and Mars (Tuesday) are "sinful" or inauspicious. Their days are not good. As stated in the *Karika* <note>a collection of verses</note>: On Sunday, the woman becomes a widow. On Monday, her children die. On Tuesday, she destroys her father-in-law. On Wednesday, she gives birth to daughters. <insert>On Thursday, she has sons, and on Friday, she has both daughters and sons.</insert> <margin>5</margin> On Saturday, she is a woman of loose character. Thus the wise know the first menses.

Furthermore, Narada <note>a legendary sage</note> says: Disease, devotion to the husband, sorrow, having sons, enjoying pleasures, being dear to the husband, and good fortune are the results of the days starting from Sunday respectively. In the *Karika*, the result for Monday is "dead children," but Narada says "devoted to the husband." To resolve this contradiction: the "dead children" result applies to Monday during the dark fortnight <note>waning moon</note>, while "devoted to the husband" applies to Monday during the bright fortnight <note>waxing moon</note>. This is the established rule. The words of Martanda <note>an authoritative astrological text</note> are preferred.

**The Sinful Occurrences** <note>original: "Papavara"</note>. This refers to the results of the lunar days. Regarding the results of the lunar days <note>Tithiphala</note>, it is said: "The Empty ones, the New Moon, the Sun, the Eighth, and the Sixth." The "Empty" days <note>Rikta</note> are the fourth, ninth, and fourteenth days of the lunar fortnight. "The New Moon" <note>Ama</note> is the fifteenth day of the dark fortnight. "The Sun" <note>Arka</note> refers to the twelfth day, as the Sun is the presiding deity of the twelfth, or because the number twelve is synonymous with the Sun. The deities of the lunar days are stated in the *Ratnamala* <note>A famous 11th-century astrological treatise by Shripati</note>: the deities of the lunar days are Brahma, the Creator, the Vishvadevas, and so on. The "Eighth" and "Sixth" are well-known. These seven lunar days are not auspicious.

Now, regarding the auspiciousness of the first menstruation <note>original: "dityah" likely referring to the woman's state</note>, it is said:

> A woman who begins her cycle on the first day is blessed; on the second, she is fortunate; on the third, she is endowed with children.
> On the fourth, she becomes a widow; on the fifth, she rests in wealth. On the sixth, she is auspicious; on the eleventh, endowed with children. On the twelfth, she is unfortunate; on the thirteenth, she bestows jewels. On the fourteenth, she will have sons; on the full moon, she will have excellent daughters. If the *Bhadra* <note>a specific astrological division of time</note> is not present during the first sight of the menses, there is no fear of theft.

Regarding the results of the location, it says: **"Earth, Earth, and another's house."** "Earth" <note>Kum</note> refers to the ground or soil; "another's house" <note>parasadanam</note> refers to a house other than her husband's home. Both of these locations are condemned. Now, regarding the inauspicious locations, it is said:

> In a temple, in a place of the ancestors <note>cremation ground</note>, in a condemned place, or in another's house. For a woman beginning her cycle, the result is the same if it occurs on a path or in the house of an outcast.

"Temple" <note>Devasthanam</note> means a house of the gods. "Place of the ancestors" <note>Pitristhanam</note> means the cremation ground. "Condemned place" means a loathsome spot. "Another's house" means a house other than the husband's. Others might ask: "In the verse, many places are mentioned, so why does the text only say 'Earth, Earth, and another's house'?" It is true. The two locations mentioned in the root text encompass everything in the verse. For example: "temple," "another's house," and "outcast's house"—these three terms are included under "another's house." The other places mentioned in the verse are included under the term "Earth" <note>Kum</note>. One might ask: "What about a path? How is a path included in 'Earth'? A path is not necessarily loathsome." It is true. However, a path has a certain impurity because of the passage of all sorts of people, as is well known in the Law Books <note>Dharmashastra</note>. Therefore, the detailed list in the previous verse might seem redundant, but here, the extra detail is given to show increasing degrees of fault. One should avoid them; thus, there is no error. <margin>Ko 2</margin>

**The Time** <note>Velakala</note> is now described: "Night, the twilights, and the afternoon." "Night" is well known. There are two "twilights" <note>dawn and dusk</note>. "Afternoon" <note>Aparahna</note> is the third part of the day when divided into three. These four times are not auspicious. Now, others are excluded... <note>text breaks off</note>

# On the Timing of First Menstruation

It has been said: A woman who begins her menses in the morning will be well-behaved, fortunate, and auspicious. If it occurs at midday, she will obtain happiness; however, if it occurs in the afternoon, she will be of loose character <note>original: "svairini" - an independent or unchaste woman</note>. If it occurs during either twilight (dawn or dusk), she will become a courtesan, and if at night, she will become a widow.

### The Influence of the Stars

Now the results of the <term>Nakshatras</term> <note>Lunar mansions or star clusters in Vedic astrology</note> are described. The stars associated with "Mitra," the "Latter" ones, and "Mula" are sharp. "Mitra" refers to the stars <term>Krittika</term> and <term>Vishakha</term>. The "Fierce" stars are the three "Prior" ones <note>Purva Phalguni, Purva Ashadha, and Purva Bhadrapada</note>, along with <term>Bharani</term> and <term>Magha</term>. 

<margin>6</margin> Regarding the "Sharp" stars—excluding <term>Mula</term>—these are <term>Ashlesha</term>, <term>Jyeshtha</term>, and <term>Ardra</term>. These ten stars are considered inauspicious. All other stars are auspicious.

As stated in the **Naradiya Samhita** <note>An ancient Sanskrit text on astrology and codes of conduct</note>:

1. **Ashwini**: If the first menses occurs under Ashwini, she will be wealthy, fortunate, blessed with sons, happy, and highly skilled in the arts.
2. **Bharani**: She may be ill-behaved, unchaste, prone to miscarriage, a lover of others, or a "crow-barren" woman <note>A woman who bears only one child</note>.
3. **Krittika** (The Fire Star): She may be sinless yet potentially unchaste, barren, prone to miscarriage, a servant, or of intoxicated intellect.
4. **Rohini** (The Brahma Star): She will be well-behaved, have good offspring, be respected, devoted to her husband, firm in her vows, and always devoted to her duties.
5. **Mrigashira** (The Moon Star): She will be virtuous, devoted to dharma (religion/duty), enduring, beloved by her husband, and rich in sons.
6. **Ardra** (The Terrible Star): She may be despised, unfortunate, miserable, lose her children, be wicked, or abandon her vows.
7. **Punarvasu** (The Aditi Star): She will be devoted to her husband, have sons, be charming to the children of others, skillful, and fond of beauty.
8. **Pushya**: She will be devoted to her husband, rich in good sons, always enjoying various pleasures, dedicated to her own work, and charitable.
9. **Ashlesha** (The Serpent Star): She may be devoted to other men's husbands, a servant, angry, merciless, lazy, subject to scandal, and have bad sons.
10. **Magha** (The Ancestor Star): She will be free from malice but prone to disease, always seeking low things, fond of her father's house, yet respected.
11. **Purva Phalguni** (The Fortune Star): She will be dedicated to others' work, poor, have bad sons, share in distress, be unkempt, harsh, and angry.
12. **Uttara Phalguni** (The Aryaman Star): She will have offspring, be devoted to dharma, free from enmity, yet perhaps lacking friends and overly attached to her mother's house.
13. **Hasta**: She will be faultless, possess great wealth, be rich in sons, enjoy life's pleasures, and be a leader or highly skilled.
14. **Chitra** (The Tvashtar Star): She will be skilled in various arts, a seeker of pleasure, clever in buying and selling, and surrounded by handsome young men.
15. **Svati** (The Wind Star): She will have many children, be skilled in crafts, have sons and grandsons, and be a virtuous woman.
16. **Vishakha**: She may be inclined toward low deeds...

It categorizes stars into auspicious and inauspicious groupings, linking celestial timing to future character traits and family outcomes.</summary>

# Page 63

If the first menstruation <note>original: prathamārtava; the onset of puberty</note> occurs in the **Hasta** <note>The 13th lunar mansion, symbolized by a hand</note> constellation, the woman will be sorrowful, sinful, yet beloved by her husband; however, she may remain childless <note>original: viputrā</note>, be of a harsh nature, and associated with the earth. 

If it occurs in the **Anuradha** <note>original: mitra bhe; the 17th lunar mansion, ruled by the deity Mitra</note> constellation, she will remain loyal to her husband's side, be well-adorned by her own family, fortunate, and blessed with many sons. **[17]**

If it occurs in **Dhanishta** <note>original: vasu; the 23rd lunar mansion</note>, even if she does not become a wanton woman <note>original: puṃścalī</note>, she may possess a restless character, have difficult children, and be of a stubborn or wretched disposition. 

If it occurs in **Mula** <note>The 19th lunar mansion, associated with the "root"</note>, she will not be endowed with the virtues necessary for raising offspring, nor will she be a source of joy; however, she will remain constantly devoted to her own duties. **[18]**

If it occurs in **Purva Ashadha** <note>original: toya; the 20th lunar mansion, associated with water</note>, she will be a secret sinner, have difficult sons, be inclined toward harming living beings, and be addicted to constant lamentation. **[19]**

If it occurs in **Uttara Ashadha** <note>original: visu bhe</note>, she will be skilled in all tasks, always devoted to righteousness <note>Dharma</note>, a joy to her family, and highly respected. **[20]**

If it occurs in **Shravana** <note>original: vishva bhe</note>, she will be rich in virtues and very fortunate. **[21]**

If it occurs in **Abhijit** <note>An intercalary lunar mansion often placed between Shravana and Dhanishta</note>, she will be endowed with sons and grandsons, possess enjoyments, wealth, and grain, be a virtuous woman <note>Sati</note>, a joy to her family, and highly respected. **[22]**

If it occurs in **Dhanishta** <note>original: vasu</note>, she will possess wealth and grain, be an enjoyer of life, endowed with sons and grandsons, devoted to her duties, and a saintly woman. **[23]**

If it occurs in **Shatabhisha** <note>original: varuṇa; the 24th lunar mansion, ruled by Varuna</note>, she will have many sons, be wealthy, devoted to her duties, a virtuous woman, a joy to her family, and highly respected. **[24]**

If it occurs in **Purva Bhadrapada** <note>original: jāṃhni bhe</note>, she will be a "kisser" <note>likely implying flirtatiousness</note>, hated by her kinsmen, always delighting in wickedness, a rogue, yet skilled in manual arts or crafts <note>original: shilpa</note>. **[25]**

If she becomes a menstruating woman <note>original: rajasvalā</note> in **Uttara Bhadrapada** <note>original: ahirbudhnye</note>, she will be wealthy, have sons, be respected, very cheerful, and beloved by her husband; she will possess the virtues of widowhood <note>note: this likely refers to the strength of character or religious austerity associated with the state, or perhaps a corruption in the text regarding long-married life</note>. **[26]**

If the first menstruation occurs in **Revati** <note>original: pauṣṇe; the 27th lunar mansion</note>, she will be firm in her vows, righteous, endowed with the happiness of having sons, famous, and full of good qualities. **[27]**

### On the Results of Junctions
Now the results of the "junction" <note>original: sandhi-phala</note> are told. This is the explanation: The junction <note>sandhi</note> refers to the gap or transition between lunar days <note>tithis</note> or constellations. It is said to be inauspicious. 

> A woman is unfortunate if the event occurs during any junction.

### On the Results of the Blood
Now the results of the blood <note>original: rakta-phala</note> are told. Regarding blood that possesses many qualities: except for that which is not reddish, blood that is "endowed with many qualities" refers to that which is neither too little nor too much. That which is not "endowed with many qualities" is blood that lacks a good, bright red color. Blood that is excessively dark or otherwise is easy to understand <note>as inauspicious</note>. Now the results of the days are spoken: The result for women regarding the first menstruation is now told according to the appearance of the menses. If the menses are white in color, she will be fortunate and endowed with sons. **[1]** One whose blood resembles the blood of a rabbit... <note>The text cuts off here</note>

If the blood resembles a precious gem <note>original: "ratnaka-sannibhe"</note>, she will give birth to both sons and daughters; if it is blue, her offspring will be stillborn. |1| If it is variegated or spotted, she herself may die; if it is reddish-yellow, the children will be stillborn. If the blood is black, the woman is destined for widowhood; thus should the menses be interpreted. |2| If the blood is thin and spotted when washed, she will be of loose character and have little vitality; if the flow is moderate, she is excellent, but if the flow is excessive, she will be unfortunate. |4|

It is also stated in another text: If the blood is deep red, a son will be born; if black, a daughter will be stillborn. If it is burnt-looking, she will be <unclear>barren</unclear>; if pale white, she will be a "crow-barren" woman <note>A "crow-barren" woman (kaka-vandhya) is a traditional term for a woman who bears only one child in her lifetime.</note>. If it is yellowish, she is said to be of loose character; if it is the color of a Gunjā seed <note>A bright red seed with a black spot, often used as a measure for gold.</note>, she will be fortunate. If it resembles vermilion, a daughter will be born. Such are the characteristics of the menstrual blood.

<term>The results of the Karana (astrological time-period):</term>
Regarding the *Karanas* starting from *Gara*: From *Gara* to *Vanija*, the *Karanas* are generally not auspicious. Now, the specific results: In *Bava*, the woman may become barren or a widow. In *Taitila*, she may have stillborn children. In *Gara*, the woman herself may perish. In *Vanija*, she will be wealthy but potentially barren. In *Shakuni* and *Chatushpada*, the woman attains widowhood. In *Naga*, she becomes a servant or has stillborn children. In *Kimstughna*, she will become a widow.

There are three types of portents <note>original: "utpata"</note> that are inauspicious. As Narada says: "The first menstruation is inauspicious if it occurs during twilight, an eclipse, or the *Vishti* period."

Now, the <term>results of the Ascendant (Lagna):</term>
Regarding the "malefic ascendant": A malefic ascendant is not favorable. These are the signs ruled by the Sun, Saturn, and Mars <note>original: "arka-yama-ara"</note>. Specifically, the malefic ascendants are Aries, Leo, Scorpio, Capricorn, and Aquarius; these signs are inauspicious. Others are auspicious. The Cancer ascendant is auspicious during the bright fortnight <note>the waxing moon</note> and inauspicious during the dark fortnight <note>the waning moon</note>, depending on the strength of its lord.

It is said: In Aries, she becomes a laborer; in Taurus, she will have sons. In Gemini, she will give birth to daughters; in Cancer, her children will die. In Leo, she attains widowhood; in Virgo, she will bear daughters. In Libra, she will have many daughters; in Scorpio, she will be devoted to wicked deeds. In Sagittarius, she will be wealthy with sons; in Capricorn, she will be miserable. In Aquarius, she will have a child only once; in Pisces, she will have few children. Narada also says: Cancer, Taurus, Sagittarius, Virgo, and Libra [are the favorable signs].

The zodiac signs are to be known as givers of auspicious results during a woman’s first menstruation <note>original: "prathamārtava"; the onset of puberty, considered a major life transition in this tradition</note>. Here too, the state of the Cancer ascendant <term>Lagna; the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at a specific moment</term> should be understood as being like the Moon.

### On the Results of Clothing
Now, the results regarding clothing are stated:
"A woman wearing bright, white, or yellow garments is excellent; otherwise, it is not good." Brightness refers to garments other than indigo, white, or yellow <note>The text suggests a specific color hierarchy where certain shades are preferred for ritual purity</note>. Various other types of clothing would not be auspicious. This is the meaning. Now, yellow clothing seen as auspicious is discussed. It is said:

-> "A woman in white clothing becomes fortunate; in red, she becomes sickly." <-
-> "A woman wearing blue at her first menstruation becomes a widow." <-
-> "One in yellow clothing becomes a seeker of enjoyment; in sturdy cloth, she is devoted to her husband." <-
-> "In yellow, she is fortunate; in beautiful clothes, she possesses grace." <-

In this matter, even if not explicitly stated here, other inauspicious factors mentioned in the earlier chapters should be considered.

### General Strength of the Ascendant
Now, the general strength of the ascendant <term>Lagna-bala</term> is stated in a summary verse:
Generally, one should avoid a cruel planet <term>Krūra-graha; typically Mars, Saturn, or the Sun</term> in the eighth house or the ascendant. Avoid a sign whose lord is "combust" <note>hidden by the Sun's light</note> or joined with cruel planets. Avoid the "wicked portion" <term>Khalāṃśa</term> and a planet situated in the eighth house. Benefic planets <term>Saumya-graha; such as Jupiter, Venus, or Mercury</term> are praised in the angles <note>1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses</note> and trines <note>1st, 5th, and 9th houses</note>. Other planets are auspicious in the sixth, eleventh, and third houses. This should be applied everywhere by understanding the specific distinctions taught.

In all matters, starting with conception <term>Garbhādhāna</term>, the Moon in certain positions should generally be avoided. Specifically, one should abandon the Moon when it is in the twelfth, eighth, sixth, or the ascendant itself. Likewise, avoid a division of the sign <term>Bhāṃśaka</term> whose lord is "blinded" or combust. This means the Navamsha <note>a 1/9th sub-division of a sign</note> of one's own ascendant has gone to an eclipsed state.

Furthermore, avoid a sign joined with any of the cruel planets. Avoid the "wicked portion" <term>Khalāṃśa</term> wherever it may fall in the ascendant or a planet. Also, avoid a planet in the eighth house from the ascendant. Regarding the movement of the ascendant: Benefics are best in the "Centers" <note>the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses</note> and "Trines" <note>the 9th and 5th houses</note>.

Also, avoid the "Death-burners" <note>Mṛtyu-tapta</note> in the ascendant. This refers to the lords of the eighth house from the birth sign and birth ascendant; if they stay in the current ascendant, they should be avoided. Other planets are praised in the "Enemy" (6th), "Gain" (11th), and "Sibling" (3rd) houses. These "other planets" refer to the malefic planets as opposed to the benefics. This logic should be applied to all results mentioned for conception and other rites, as it is a general rule. How so?

<page-num>8</page-num>

How are the specific rituals to be understood? Having known the unchanging nature of these specific rituals, the author now describes the **Conception Ritual** <note>Sanskrit: *Garbhadhana*; the first of the sixteen life-cycle sacraments intended to purify the procreative act</note> in the *Sragdhara* meter:

-> After the fourth day of the wife's period, avoiding even days, funeral anniversaries, and the like, and having abandoned the constellations of Magha, Mula, and Revati, as well as the birth star... O seeker of a son, go to your wife when Jupiter, the Sun, and the Moon are in the Ascendant in odd signs and divisions; but for a daughter, let the Moon be strong. <-

The meaning is: "O seeker of a son, being joyful, go to your own wife during the fertile period." The term "seeker of a son" refers to one who desires male offspring. "During the fertile period" refers to the sixteen nights starting from the first appearance of the menses. Being "joyful" implies that the husband is happy and the wife is likewise pleased and fortunate. As **Manu** <note>The legendary lawgiver and author of the *Manusmriti*</note> says: *The natural fertile period of women is said to be sixteen nights.*

What should be avoided? Having abandoned the first four nights <gloss>four</gloss>, the even days, the "intervals" <note>astrological gaps or transitions</note>, and the anniversary of the parents' death (*Shraddha*). This means one should avoid the first four nights of the cycle. One should also avoid the constellations of **Mula**, **Magha**, and **Revati**. Furthermore, one must avoid the 8th lunar day (*Vasu*), the 14th lunar day (*Kali*), and the individual's birth star (*Janma-nakshatra*).

Additionally, one must avoid the *Parvan* days <note>New Moon and Full Moon</note>. One should go to the wife only after avoiding the New Moon, Full Moon, the astronomical conjunctions of *Vyatipata* and *Vaidhriti*, the Sun's entry into a new sign (*Sankranti*), and other great ill-omened periods.

As **Narada** <note>A divine sage and author of legal and astrological texts</note> says: *From the appearance of the menses, the first four days are for purification; thereafter, this rule applies to all actions.*

**Vashistha** <note>One of the seven great sages (Saptarishi)</note> states: *One should avoid approaching one's wife during eclipses, the ill-omened Vyatipata and Vaidhriti yogas, the first half of the day, twilights, the Parvan days, and the anniversary of the parents' death. On even days, avoiding Magha and Mula, the seeker of a son should approach his virtuous wife. On odd days, the seeker of a daughter should do so, having abandoned the forbidden lunar days and ascendants.*

In the *Jyotisharka* <note>A classic astrological text, "The Sun of Astrology"</note>: *One should go to his wife on even nights for a virtuous son, avoiding Magha and Mula, and abandoning the censured 8th and 14th lunar days.*

Under what planetary conditions? When **Jupiter** <note>original: "Devejya" - the teacher of the gods</note>, the **Sun** <note>Arka</note>, and the **Moon** <note>Indu</note> are in the **Ascendant** <note>Lagna</note> and situated in **odd signs or divisions** <note>Visha-bhata-vage; odd signs like Aries or Gemini are considered masculine in Vedic astrology</note>.

<summary>This page details the astrological and ritual requirements for the Garbhadhana (conception) ceremony, citing authorities like Manu and Vashistha to define auspicious timing based on lunar days, constellations, and planetary positions.</summary>

By being in odd divisions: this refers to the birth falling in an odd <term>Navamsha</term> <note>a 1/9th division of a zodiac sign</note>. This means being situated in an odd sign and an odd ninth-part. Not only by odd divisions, but also by those possessing strength. It is said that when these factors are strong, the result is as follows. Now, he explains the specific rules for those desiring a daughter. O maiden! By all the characteristics just mentioned, if at that time the planets are in even signs and even divisions, a female birth is predicted.

As stated in the *Brihat Jataka* <note>a foundational 6th-century Sanskrit text on astronomy and horoscopy by Varahamihira</note>: "If the Ascendant, the Sun, Jupiter, and the Moon are powerful and situated in odd signs and masculine ninth-parts, a male birth is predicted; if they are in even signs and even ninth-parts, a female birth occurs." This applies when there is a "Good Moon"—meaning when the Moon is transitively strong. The phrase "Good Moon" should be applied to every auspicious timing until the end of this book. Thus is the strength of the Ascendant.

<insert>Now, he speaks in the *Sragdhara* meter regarding the Pumsavana and Simantonnayana rituals:</insert>
<insert>"In a masculine asterism, on the days of Mars, Jupiter, or the Sun; in an odd sign and odd ninth-part (excluding Gemini), the Pumsavana rite should occur. The Simanta rite is also auspicious when the 12th and 8th houses are free of malefic planets, and not on a Full/New Moon or 'Empty' lunar days. The first rite is in the 2nd or 3rd month, the second in the 6th month; the planetary strength of the male embryo must be applied to both."</insert>

The first half of the verse: <insert>1</insert> In a masculine <term>Nakshatra</term> <note>lunar mansion</note>. These masculine asterisms are: Pushya, Hasta, Punarvasu, Abhijit, Shravana, Ashwini, and Anuradha. Thus it is said. Likewise, the Bhadrapadas. On the days of the Sun, Jupiter, or Mars (Sunday, Thursday, or Tuesday). In an odd sign and odd ninth-part, excluding Gemini. This means in any odd-numbered <term>Lagna</term> <note>Ascendant</note> and its corresponding ninth-part, provided it is not Gemini. Both the <term>Pumsavana</term> <note>a ritual to "bring forth a male" performed during pregnancy</note> and the <term>Simantonnayana</term> <note>the "parting of the hair" ritual for the mother's well-being</note> become auspicious.

How should the houses be? When the 12th and 8th houses are "good," meaning free from the presence of malefic planets. On a lunar day that is not a <term>Parva</term> <note>New/Full Moon or eclipse days</note> or a <term>Rikta</term> <note>"Empty" days: the 4th, 9th, or 14th of the lunar fortnight</note>. Regarding the months: the first rite, Pumsavana, is performed in the second or third month from conception.

# On the Timing of Pregnancy Rituals

It shall be so. Furthermore, the **Simantonnayana** <note>The "parting of the hair" ritual performed for the prosperity of the expectant mother and the fetus</note> <gloss>4</gloss> should take place in the fourth, <gloss>6</gloss> sixth, <gloss>8</gloss> eighth, <gloss>9</gloss> or ninth months. <gloss>Now, regarding the speech in me...</gloss> The strength of the Ascendant <term>Lagna: the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the time of the ritual</term> and the planetary houses has been described; that same strength, as mentioned by the sages, should be auspicious for these two: the **Pumsavana** <note>A ritual performed to ensure the birth of a male child or a healthy child</note> and the **Simantonnayana**. <gloss>The bright fortnight of the moon is auspicious. Not the dark fortnight.</gloss> Here, regarding the rituals starting from conception, it will be stated later that one need not worry about the combustion of Jupiter or Venus. The strength of the Ascendant is required everywhere. According to the sage **Jatukarnya**: The **Pumsavana** should occur in either the second or third month. <gloss>Now, if there is an illness, it should be performed along with the Simantonnayana.</gloss> **Nrsimha** states: Avoiding the "Empty" days <term>Rikta: the 4th, 9th, and 14th lunar days, considered unlucky for new ventures</term> and the eve of the new moon, the days for **Pumsavana** are auspicious. 

<margin>And so it is.</margin>

The wise should perform the **Simantonnayana** when the Moon is in the constellations of Magha or Mula. One should perform the **Pumsavana** on the days of Mars, Jupiter, or the Sun. **Brihaspati** says: <gloss>Leaving aside Cancer, Gemini, and Virgo, the remaining signs are auspicious.</gloss> **Narada** states: In the fourth, sixth, or eighth month, when the lord of that month is endowed with strength, and when the couple possesses the strength of the Moon and the stars. It should be done on a non-"Empty" day, on a Tuesday, Thursday, or Sunday. Avoiding the "Friend" and "Fierce" constellations, it should be done in a masculine Ascendant and a masculine division of the zodiac <term>Navamsha: a one-ninth division of a sign used for precision in astrology</term>. <gloss>In the 6th or 8th house from the birth Ascendant, or in the 8th house from the ritual Ascendant, it is not auspicious. If joined or aspected by a benefic planet, or even if joined by a malefic planet, it is beneficial in the 5th, 6th, 4th, 2nd, or 11th houses.</gloss> For women, the **Simantonnayana** is auspicious during the first pregnancy <gloss>when benefic planets are in the 5th, 9th, or center houses</gloss>. When malefic planets are present and the Moon is in the 8th house, it should be avoided. <gloss>If even one cruel planet is in the 12th, 5th, or 8th house from the Ascendant, it undoubtedly destroys the pregnant woman or the fetus.</gloss> 

Now, regarding the **Jatakarma** <note>Birth rites performed immediately after delivery</note>, the author speaks in the *Sragdhara* meter: <insert>The father should perform the birth rite for the newborn; here, there is no delay due to the period of impurity.</insert> <insert>It should be performed before the umbilical cord is cut, or after the period of impurity, or on the 20th or 22nd day.</insert> On the day of birth, avoiding the "Empty" days, the 8th lunar day, the night, and the afternoon. <gloss>When a child is born, one should perform the Jatakarma.</gloss> In this matter of **Jatakarma**, there is no "other impurity" <term>Ashaucha: ritual pollution following a birth or death</term> for the father. <gloss>This means: even if a birth or death occurs simultaneously, in the context of this birth rite, the fault of ritual impurity does not apply.</gloss> <gloss>Even when other impurities exist, if a son is born...</gloss>

# On the Birth Rituals and Naming Ceremonies

The father should perform a full immersion bath while still wearing his clothes <note>original: "sachailam snanam"; a ritual requirement for sudden purification upon hearing of a birth</note>, following the specific rules of his own household tradition. The term "of the father" indicates he is the primary agent of the ritual. This should be performed during auspicious constellations.

The constellations recommended are:
*   **Swift/Light:** Ashwini, Hasta, and Pushya.
*   **Fixed:** Rohini and the three "Uttara" stars (Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, Uttara Bhadrapada).
*   **Movable:** Shravana, Dhanishta, Shatabhisha, Punarvasu, and Swati.
*   **Soft/Gentle:** Anuradha, Revati, Mrigashirsha, and Chitra.

The strength of the rising sign (Lagna) should also be considered. It is stated: "Even at the moment of birth, or at the end of the birth-impurity period, the wise should perform the **Birth Ritual** (*Jatakarman*) preceded by honoring the ancestors and virtuous people." The sage **Prajapati** says: "The eternal Birth Ritual should be performed when Jupiter or Venus are in a central house (Kendra) in the first, fourth, seventh, or tenth positions."

Regarding birth-impurity (*Sutaka*):
When a son is born during a period of impurity, the father attains immediate purification for the purpose of the ritual; he is purified from the impurity specifically to perform the rite. This is the view of **Samvarta**. Upon the birth of a son, the father's bath with clothes on is ordained. This bath should be performed with cold water. On this, **Jabali** says: "Performing a bath for a specific occasion (Naimittika) or for a desired result (Kamya) should be done with cold water."

This may be performed even at night. **Vasistha** states: "At the birth of a son, during a sacrifice, at the sun's transition (Sankranti), or during an eclipse, a bath at night is highly recommended; otherwise, one should not bathe at night." This bath should be performed facing <margin>measured</margin> North. It is further said: "Having heard that a son is born, the father should bathe with his clothes on, facing North, in a river or a natural pond."

If one is unable to go to a river at night, **Samkhyayana** provides a special rule: "Water drawn during the day and touched with gold becomes pure; if a night-bath is required, one should bathe near such water with sesame seeds."

### The Naming Ceremony (Namakarana)

Now, the Naming Ceremony is described. They should perform the naming ceremony on the tenth, twelfth, or eighteenth day. The different classes (Varnas) starting with the Brahmins should perform it as follows:
*   **Brahmins:** On the 10th day.
*   **Kshatriyas:** On the 16th day.
*   **Vaishyas:** On the 20th day.
*   **Shudras:** On the 22nd day.

There are other options provided by the sages. **Vyasa** says: "Some teachers desire the naming on the 10th day. Others on the 12th. Some say at the end of the month, and other wise men say on the 18th day."

Regarding inauspicious times:
Even during difficult astrological periods like *Vyatipata*, *Sankranti* (solar transit), or an eclipse, one should avoid these for the Naming Ceremony unless it is for a funeral rite (*Shraddha*). <unclear>Here</unclear>, if the time of a solar transit or a specific lunar day occurs, one should perform it without delay, as there is no fault in doing so for a newborn.

The constellations for the Naming Ceremony are the same as those for the Birth Ritual: the "Auspicious," "Swift," and "Fixed" stars. <insert>According to the requirements of place, time, and materials...</insert>

# Page 10

If the proper time has passed, one should perform the ritual briefly during the Northern Transit of the Sun <note>Sanskrit: *Uttarāyana*, the six-month period when the sun moves north, considered auspicious</note>, during the waxing moon, on a fixed lunar mansion, on a day with a favorable planet, and when the strength of the Moon and stars is sufficient for both the child and the father.

### On Prohibited Times
Now, the specific times to be avoided are stated: "Avoid the movable ascendant..." One should avoid the movable ascendant <term>Lagna: the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon</term>, the "Empty" lunar days <note>original: *Riktā*, the 4th, 9th, and 14th days of the lunar fortnight</note>, the 8th day, Tuesday <note>original: *Āra*</note>, and Saturday <note>original: *Manda*</note>. Having avoided these, one should act according to the strength of the planets, whether they are gentle or harsh. Nighttime and the afternoon (the third part of the day) are also generally avoided. The strength of the Ascendant is paramount in all matters.

As **Brihaspati** <note>An ancient sage and authority on law and astrology</note> says: "The morning is best, midday is middling, and the afternoon and movable signs should be avoided for the naming ceremony." **Nrsimha** states: "One should not perform it in the evening, during bad astrological combinations, on Saturdays, on the days of the Sun or Mars, on 'Empty' days, on the 8th day, or during the *Kinstughna* period <note>an inauspicious astrological division</note>. Even at night, it should not be done if these faults are present." **Brihaspati** further notes: "On the days of Mercury, Thursday, Friday, and Sunday, the naming brings prosperity. Fixed zodiac signs are best, and dual-natured signs are acceptable if joined by Mercury." **Nrsimha** adds: "Planets in the 8th house from the Ascendant are not auspicious for the naming ceremony."

Regarding whose names should be established, the *Sangraha* states: "The naming ceremony is ordained for temples, elephants, horses, adopted sons, wells, all types of instruments, monuments, women, men, poetic works, palaces, and royal decrees."

### On Placing the Child in the Cradle
**Now, the placing in the cradle,** the first sitting on the ground, the first feeding of milk, and the first outing—these four are discussed. On the twelfth day from birth, or if that time has passed, under the constellations of Shravana, the "soft" stars, or the "light" stars, the child should be placed in the cradle. This should be done on a day of a gentle planet, in the fifth month, or on the thirty-first day. The first outing from the house is said to occur in the third month.

On the twelfth day after birth, the child should be placed in the cradle <note>original: *palaka* or *khatva*</note>. If the time has passed, use the Shravana constellation or the soft and light constellations. The strength of the Ascendant is always vital. As stated by **Brihaspati**: "The mounting of the bed should be done on the tenth, twelfth, sixteenth, or twenty-second day." In the **Bhavishya Purana**: "On a desired auspicious day, with the strength of the Moon and stars, under soft or Shravana constellations, the mother or family women, having meditated on the Sleeping Lord <note>Vishnu</note>, should place the child with the head facing East."

### On Sitting on the Ground
**Regarding the first sitting on the ground:** Under the constellations of Mrigashira, Ashwini, Pushya, Shravana, Purva-ashadha, Purva-phalguni, the "Uttara" stars, Rohini, or Jyeshtha, on an auspicious day (excluding "Empty" days), in the fifth month from birth, one should place the child on the ground with sacred chants. This means the ritual of sitting. As stated in the **Matsya Purana**: "In the fifth month, one should seat him upon the earth." <meta>catchword: Rama</meta>

# Page 57

In this matter, all planets are considered auspicious, but Mars is especially favored here. Those days devoid of specific lunar dates <note>Tithis</note> and those falling in the "two-kośa" range are said to be beneficial. The three "Uttara" stars, the gentle stars, Pushya, Hasta, and those ruled by the Sun are recommended. The stars of Prajapati and Hasta are excellent, as are Ashvini and Anuradha. One should worship the Lord in his Boar incarnation <note>Varaha</note>, the Earth, and likewise the Brahmins. Having smeared the area in front of the vessel of sacred ash and making it wide, the child, having been bathed, should be seated upon that ash with the pleasant sound of musical instruments. Both the verses for giving are stated there. Thus, may there be purity, omnipresent long life, and prosperity for all, and may he be dear to everyone.

**Regarding the Drinking of Milk**
On the thirty-first day: On the thirty-first day after birth, the presentation of the son occurs <gloss>meaning, one should feed him with one's own milk.</gloss> **Now, according to Narasimha:** On the thirty-first day itself, one should provide milk using a conch shell. Now, for a mother with little milk, he states an alternative ritual: **By the stars mentioned for first feeding.** That is, one may also use the constellations prescribed for the *Annaprashana* <note>the first feeding of solid food</note>; this is an indicative rule. <insert>The rules mentioned for Annaprashana</insert> and other factors should also be considered. What are the characteristics? Rahu <note>the eclipse node</note> should not be facing the direction of the sun or the ascendant; this means one should avoid the direction where Rahu's mouth is turned. When the Ascendant is strong, it is good in every way. **The mention of "Fire and others" is clearly an indicative list.** Therefore, they say that the directions of Rudra, the Yoginis, and so on must also be avoided. In the *Jyotir-nibandha*: One should avoid the face of the Yogini, Rahu, and Rudra. Such are the characteristics of the stars. In the directions of Indra, Vayu, Yama, and Rudra, as well as the stars of Water and Fire; for half a watch <note>approx. 1.5 hours</note> at sunrise... <margin>3</margin> ...the eight directions. "From sunrise" means from the moment the sun crests the horizon. The characteristic of Rudra: Indra, the Moon, Fire, the Demon, Yama, Varuna, Vayu, and Agni—the Goddess Shiva wanders through these directions every two ghatikas <note>approx. 48 minutes</note> from sunrise; "Hari" here refers to Indra.

**Now he describes the First Outing <note>Nishkramana</note>**
As stated in one's own household sutras, the outing occurs in the fourth month. Now, in the fourth month, the meaning of this is: the first exit from the house. It is like a journey. It is "stated," meaning it was spoken by the ancients. In this section on planetary combinations, that should be applied. Regarding the outing, Brihaspati said: <insert>Now, regarding the outing, if one leaves the house at an improper time</insert> <sig>57</sig> it results in the destruction of the child's lifespan and prosperity—so says Yama. One should perform the outing in the fourth solar month. In the *Karika*: The outing should be done in the fourth month or under the Pushya star. After a bath, well-adorned, and having twice performed the ritual for well-being, one should take the child from the house and worship at a temple. Having properly worshipped the deity, one should have the child's blessings recited. Having circumambulated the house, the father should bring the child to his own quarters. Then, having gone to the mother's quarters, one should take the child to the maternal uncle's house. By their words of blessing, the child is greeted with long life. In the opinion of Jayanta, this child-outing ritual was not written down here. One should apply it according to one's own household traditions.

There, the piercing should be performed... some, however, do not observe the days of Saturn, Mars, or the Sun...
