<meta>This page continues the commentary on the Muhurta Martanda, a classical text on Vedic astrology (Muhurta). It focuses on the specific timing and rituals for two important life-cycle rites (Samskaras): Karnavedhana (ear-piercing) and Annaprashana (the first feeding of solid food).</meta>

<margin>Commentary on the Muhurta Martanda</margin> Regarding the stars Vaishva <note>Uttara Ashadha</note>, Ashvini, and Vasu <note>Dhanishta</note>, and the lunar days (Tithis), one should avoid the three junctions of the day <note>dawn, noon, and sunset</note>. For the son of a virtuous person, the ears should be pierced when Jupiter is in the ascendant, according to the number of letters in the name. Regarding the "friendly" stars: Anuradha, Revati, Mrigashirsha, and Chitra; and the "swift" stars: Pushya, Abhijit, and Hasta. If the piercing is done under Varuna <note>Shatabhisha</note> or Punarvasu, it should be in an odd-numbered year of the child's life, starting from the third year. The months of Pausha, Ashadha, and Chaitra are mentioned. "Ka" refers to Karttika, "Po" to Pausha, and "Shu" to Shuchi <note>Ashadha</note>. These are the times for the son.

<margin>11</margin> One should pierce the ears of the son. Regarding the method for males and females: with Jupiter in a strong position, the piercing should be done by a skilled person so that it is successful. As it is said regarding the tenth day and so on, the piercing should be done.

<margin>12</margin> For women, the left ear is pierced first. This procedure is described in the supplementary texts. Now, regarding the ear-piercing in the third or fifth year, in the months of Pushya or Ashadha <unclear>it is said</unclear> in Revati. On a Sunday, having seated the child, one consecrates the right ear with the mantra "Auspicious may we hear with our ears" <note>Rig Veda 1.89.8: Bhadram karnebhih shrunuyama devah</note>. Then the left ear is pierced while the child is spoken to sweetly, followed by a feast for the Brahmins. When the child is well-dressed and decorated with garlands, and Jupiter is in the ascendant (Lagna). What should be avoided? The days of the Sun, Mars, and Saturn, which are harsh. The "Amsha" refers to the nine-fold division (Navamsha). The "Vara" are the well-known days of the week. One should also avoid the "Empty" lunar days (Rikta Tithis): the 4th, 9th, and 14th. Also avoid the 11th (Ekadashi) and 8th (Ashtami). Furthermore, avoid the two junctions of day and night (twilight), as the strength of the ascendant is paramount.

On this matter, Nrisimha says: The ears should be pierced in an odd year of the child's life. In the bright fortnight, on an auspicious day, in the months of Chaitra, Pausha, or Karttika. The 11th, 8th, and the "Empty" days should be avoided by the wise. All other lunar days are acceptable. Agastya states: One should not perform ear-piercing during the two twilights. At night, certain portions of the day and certain week-days are to be avoided. Jupiter, Venus, and the Moon should be in favorable positions. In the *Sangraha*, it is said: needles made of gold, silver, or even iron are auspicious according to one's wealth. In a beautiful courtyard, in a clean place, under a favorable Sun, the ears of women and men (left and right respectively) should be pierced. One should pierce with a copper needle. After the piercing, the area should be cleansed with <margin>11</margin> good water. Thus says Devala: By the piercing of the ear-hole, the sins of the past are dissolved.

Now, regarding the first feeding of grain (Annaprashana): it is prescribed in the sixth or eighth month for a boy. <unclear>It is said</unclear> in the fifth or seventh month for a girl. This should be done when the Moon is strong, avoiding the "Empty" days. The child should be placed in the center of the assembly. For a male child, the feeding is prescribed in the sixth or eighth month from birth. For a girl, it is in the fifth or seventh month. <margin>Rama</margin>

It is stated here that <gloss>indeed</gloss> <insert>the</insert> Hari, Vasu, and Rikta days are where the sages dwell; this is the distinction. One should avoid the "Nanda" days (1st, 6th, 11th lunar days), the "Ashtami" (8th), the "Ekadashi" (11th), and the "Avama" (a day where three lunar dates overlap). "Hari" refers to the 11th, "Vasu" to the 8th, and "Rikta" to the 4th, 9th, and 14th lunar days. Some <note>astrologers</note> also avoid the birth star (<term>Janma Nakshatra</term> - the constellation the moon was in at birth). In which decan (<term>Drekkana</term> - a 10-degree division of a zodiac sign)? The time is determined by the decan and the ninth-division (<term>Navamsha</term>) of the day. This is the combination: when the planets are in their respective decans. What kind of decan? One that is not "pierced" or afflicted. One should know the loss of the precise time through the ninth-division.

Furthermore, in which signs should one proceed? In the signs of Gemini, Virgo, Libra, Taurus, and Aquarius. Aries, Scorpio, and Pisces are well-known as unfavorable for the first feeding of solid food (<term>Annaprashana</term>). When should this occur? When the thoughts are pure and the "Centers" (<term>Kendras</term> - the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses of a horoscope) are strong, as well as the 5th, 9th, and 11th houses. If the Moon is full and the Ascendant (<term>Lagna</term>) is strong and free from the influence of "cruel" planets (malefics), then the feeding should take place.

<insert>Narada says:</insert> For a boy, the feeding should be in the 6th or 8th month; for a girl, in the 5th or 7th month. Avoiding the Rikta days and the three days starting with Nanda, the remaining lunar dates are excellent for the feeding on auspicious weekdays.

<insert>The Elder Narada says:</insert> In the ceremonies of piercing the ears, putting on new clothes, the first feeding, and the sacred thread ceremony, the birth star is considered auspicious for the performance of the ritual. When Jupiter, Mercury, or Venus are in the decan of the day, it is praised for the feeding. The signs of Gemini, Taurus, Aquarius, Libra, Virgo, Leo, Cancer, and Capricorn are praised; Aries, Pisces, and Scorpio are not.

<insert>Narada says:</insert> Use the movable, fixed, soft, and swift constellations (<term>Nakshatras</term>), but avoid the "death" (<term>Naidhana</term>) constellation.

<insert>Shripati says:</insert> Regarding the Sun in the Ascendant and so on: if there is an absence of Jupiter or Venus due to their "combustion" (<term>Maudhya</term> - being too close to the sun to be seen), or if they are in their "infancy" or "old age" (the first or last degrees of a sign), should the work not be done? In the four rituals starting with the first feeding, one must avoid the combustion of Jupiter and Venus. One should also avoid the southern transit of the sun (<term>Dakshinayana</term>), the intercalary month (<term>Mala-masa</term>), and when the Sun is in Leo or the Guru (Jupiter) is in Leo.

Regarding the rituals from conception (<term>Garbhadhana</term>) to the first feeding: one must clearly avoid the combustion of Jupiter and Venus because these are times restricted by the scriptures. Why the restriction? One must avoid the "infancy" and "old age" of Jupiter and Venus because they lack strength during those periods. One should not necessarily avoid the southern transit or the intercalary month <unclear>loss</unclear> for all rites, but for these specific ones, the restriction applies. When the Sun is in Leo or Jupiter is in Leo or Virgo, these are to be avoided. All this should be understood by the wise as the truth. At the time of birth itself...

# Commentary on the Market of Auspicious Moments
## Page 12

That prescribed action should be performed; one should not worry about the "setting" <note>astrological invisibility or combustion</note> of planets if it causes a delay beyond the prescribed time. Regarding the **First Feeding of Solids** <note>Annaprasana</note>, it is prescribed in the sixth or eighth month, or within a two-month window. In that case, it should be performed in whichever month the "blindness" <note>Modhya: the period when Jupiter or Venus are too close to the sun</note> of planets does not occur.

If, due to the nature of the time and the required action, there is a blockage <note>astrological obstruction</note> during those two months—even if it lasts twenty-five days—it should not be considered here. Thus, one should deliberate on the rites starting from **Conception** <note>Garbhadhana</note> up to the **First Feeding**.

<margin>12</margin> Because the tradition <note>Smriti</note> remembers: "One should perform daily and occasional rites through other means." The sequence of the **Sacraments** <note>Samskaras: life-cycle rites</note> is stated in the *Katyayana Grihya Karika*: **Conception**, **Quickening of a male child** <note>Pumsavana</note>, **Parting of the hair** <note>Simantonnayana</note>, then the **Birth-rite** <note>Jatakarma</note>, the **First Outing**,

<margin>12</margin> and the **First Feeding**; in order, the **Tonsure** <note>Chuda/Chaula: first haircut</note>, the **Initiation** <note>Upanayana</note>, the four **Vedic Vows**, the **Gift of the Cow** <note>Godana</note>, the **Loosening of the Girdle**, and **Marriage**—these are the subsequent rites. In this sequence, for the rites ending with the First Feeding, one should look for the "blindness" of Jupiter and Venus.

As **Brihaspati** <note>The divine preceptor and authority on law/astrology</note> says: "In the month suited for the action, if Jupiter or Venus are 'set' <note>invisible</note>, it does not cause a defect; the month's strength is what makes it powerful." **Yama** says: "In the case of a death or a birth, for the previous monthly rites, or for daily rites, one should not avoid the intercalary month <note>Adhimasa: an extra month added to the lunar calendar</note>." **Bhrigu** states: "The rites of Parting the Hair, Birth-rites, and those ending with <insert>Feeding</insert> should be done in sequence; there is no fault even if Jupiter has entered Leo <note>an usually inauspicious transit</note>." In the **Katyayana Smriti**: "The rites from Conception to the First Feeding may be done in an impure month <note>Malimlucha/intercalary month</note>." Bhaskara says: "It is better to do them earlier as prescribed." <term>Et cetera</term> <note>original: "ityadi"</note>. One should avoid the "setting" of planets for rites starting with the **Tonsure**.

<insert>Now the definition of "Childhood" and "Old Age" of planets is stated.</insert> Regarding Jupiter and Venus: "Childhood" or "Old Age" refers to the seven days after they rise or the seven days before they set. There are many views on this, but the "middle path" of seven days is accepted here.

<insert>Now the prohibition regarding the Entrance into Study is stated.</insert> Regarding the "blindness" at the time of entrance: "Entrance" refers to the Master of the Vedas <note>the student beginning study</note>. One should not perform the **Vow of the Girdle** <note>Maunji Bandhana/Upanayana</note> or the **Beginning of Vedic Study** during the "blindness" of the planets. If these vows are performed during such a time, the boy will become a fool. One might ask: "It makes sense that starting the Vedas during an inauspicious time leads to foolishness, but why the Vow?" It is said: because the study of the Rigveda and other actions are performed starting from the Vow-binding. Therefore, foolishness can result there too.

As **Yajnavalkya** <note>A famous legal and philosophical sage</note> says: "The teacher, having initiated the pupil, should teach him the Veda preceded by the Great Mystical Utterances <note>Maha-vyahriti: Bhur, Bhuvah, Svah</note>, and should teach him rules of cleanliness and conduct. For each Veda, the student-vow lasts twelve years, or five years, or until it is mastered. Some say the **Tonsure** <note>Keshanta</note> occurs in the sixteenth year. Through these austerities, vows, and actions, the Veda alone is the highest means of liberation for the twice-born." And so says the *Katyayana Grihya*: "Having finished the entire Veda <insert>22</insert> through austerities, he should bathe <note>the Samavartana ceremony</note>." Some say the service to the teacher lasts forty or twelve years.

Now, in two verses in the *Upajati* meter, he speaks of the **Tonsure**: "The Tonsure should be done in the five months starting with Magha <note>approx. Jan-June</note>, in the bright fortnight, on the 2nd, 3rd, 5th, 8th... if the mother is not pregnant. If it is done along with the **Initiation**, then this restriction <note>regarding the mother's pregnancy</note> does not apply. The Veda, the Vow, and the Initiation should be done when the moon and planets are very pure..." <note>The text transitions into technical astrological placements for the haircutting ceremony</note>.

<meta>This page continues the discussion of ritual timing for life-cycle ceremonies (Samskaras), specifically focusing on the astrological and familial conditions required for the first haircut (Tonsure) and the initiation ceremony (Sacred Thread).</meta>

Avoid the days of the spiritual teacher and the student, the end of the night, and the empty lunar days. **Tonsure** <note>original: "Chaul/Chudakarana"; the ritual shaving of a child's head</note> should be performed in the five months beginning with Magha <note>roughly January to June</note>. It is stated that it should be done when the sun is in the northern course and the lunar fortnight is bright. Thus it was said by **Narada**: "In the five months starting with Magha, avoid the rainy season and perform the Tonsure in the bright half." It should be done in the second, third, fifth, or eighth year, according to the custom of one's own family.

And it was said by **Katyayana**: "The Tonsure of the hair is to be done in the third year, or as promised, or even up to the sixteenth year for the cutting of the hair <note>original: "Keshanta"</note>, as is auspicious or according to all customs." **Harihara Mishra** explained this as referring to the passing of the years.

And so say the teachers and students: "Some perform it in the first year, others in the third or fifth. Or it may be performed together with the **Sacred Thread Ceremony** <note>original: "Upanayana"</note>; these are the options based on family tradition."

If the mother is pregnant, this ritual should not be performed. Now, regarding the stated years (2nd, 3rd, 5th, or 8th), if the mother of the boy is pregnant, then this Tonsure should not be performed. However, if the child is over five years old, it may be performed even if she is pregnant. This is the established rule.

<gloss>Now, Narada says:</gloss> "One should not have the Tonsure performed while the mother is bathing <note>referring to the post-menstrual or post-partum bath</note>. Before the age of five, one should not perform it if she is pregnant; after five, it may be done even if she is pregnant."

If it is performed together with the Sacred Thread ceremony, then the restriction regarding the mother's pregnancy does not apply, as there is no fault in that case. So it was said by the same authority: "If one performs it along with the initiation, then no fault exists."

<margin>Jump</margin>

For one seeking welfare, these three should be avoided until the end of the period. "Avoid" means one should not perform the ritual.

<gloss>And so in the Laws of Manu:</gloss> <note>original: "Manusmriti"</note> "If the mother is in her menses <note>original: "Rajasvala"</note> during a wedding, a vow, or a Tonsure, then the auspicious ceremony should be performed only after she is purified."

If an auspicious moment <note>term: Muhurta; a specific astrological window of time</note> cannot be found, a rite of pacification <note>term: Shantika; a ritual to offset negative influences</note> is prescribed. [The text describes a ritual involving a golden image of Brahma, the recitation of the Sri Sukta hymn, and offerings of milk-rice (Payasam) to remove the "taint of the menses" so the ritual may proceed.]

What is the meaning of "Udakya"? It means a woman in her menstrual period.

Now, the specific days and stars according to the different classes <note>term: Varnas; the four social classes of ancient India</note>:
The ritual should be performed when the Ascendant is strong. For the four classes starting with the **Brahmins**, the Tonsure should be performed respectively on Sunday, Tuesday, and Saturday.
- **Ina** means the Sun (Sunday).
- **Ara** means Mars (Tuesday).
- **Arki** means Saturn (Saturday).

This is what is being said: For a Brahmin, it should be on Sunday; for a Kshatriya, on Tuesday; for a Vaishya, on Saturday; and for Shudras, also on Saturday.

The twelve sacraments <note>term: Samskaras; rites of passage</note> are mentioned here. [The text lists various rites including Vedic vows and initiation.]

Regarding the moon: Monday is praised during the bright fortnight, but it is considered censurable during the dark fortnight. Wednesday is excellent here, even if associated with "malefic" planets.

<gloss>Here, Narada says:</gloss> "On the empty lunar dates, the Sun and Moon days are auspicious." **Brihaspati** says: "Monday is good in the bright half, but despised in the dark half. Wednesday is declared auspicious even when joined with a malefic planet. The days of malefic planets are auspicious for Brahmins when the Sun is favorable..."

<meta>This page continues the technical commentary on the "Muhurtamartanda" (The Sun of Auspicious Moments), specifically the "Dipika" (The Lamp) commentary. # The Lamp of the Sun of Auspicious Moments
## Page 12

<margin>
Mu. Ma. Di.
12
13
</margin>

**For Vaishyas and Shudras**, the ritual should be performed with **auspicious actions** <note>original: "shubhankritikkaih"</note> under the constellations of Revati, Ashwini, Punarvasu, Pushya, Hasta, and Jyeshtha. **The ancient days are said to be** Revati and Ashwini, **followed by Punarvasu, Pushya, Arka (Sunday/Hasta), and Jyeshtha. With the Moon in the fortresses (signs), again with** the Sun, Hari (Shravana), and the three... meaning **with the three constellations starting from Hasta and the three starting from Shravana.** <note>This refers to groups of three stars used to calculate timing.</note> **And so says Narada: "The second, the Moon, the Ashwins (Ashwini), the Sun, the Moon, and the Mind (constellations)..."** in the ritual of **Tonsure (Chaula)**, **"Hasta, Ardra, Chitra, and the auspicious Aditi (Punarvasu)."**

These are the **shaven ones** <note>referring to planets or signs that prohibit shaving</note> in this context of Tonsure. **Arka is the Sun, Shveta is the Moon, the seventh from Arka is Saturn, and Arka is well-known.** These four are called **"Munda" (shaven/bald), which is the fourth general result mentioned.** Specifically, a special result is stated: **"If the Sun or Saturn is in the seventh house, death occurs; likewise if in the eighth. In one's own place, it causes the destruction of people; in the ninth house, it brings misfortune."** Here, even the fourth house is mentioned as having a general result, which is well-known in the world; **by the maxim of "the higher authority," there is no fault.**

Therefore, all planets in the eighth house are "shaven" (inauspicious). **On this, Brihaspati says: "All planets in the eighth house are censurable, except for Venus. If Venus is in the eighth house and the Sun is present, it bestows all wealth upon the child."** Even if they are malefic, they become desired. Here, in the **eighth house**, these sacrifices occur; being of such a kind, they are without blemish. In the eighth or eleventh, they are auspicious. **Narada says: "In Tonsure and First Feeding (Annaprashana), the gentle planets are praised even in the eighth or twelfth houses."** Here, regarding the loss of knowledge, **"except for the scriptures" is his own statement.** Another point is the "newness" in this way. **The position of the hidden (planets), the "Empty" (Rikta) lunar days, and the twilight—not the well-known twilight, but the night.** These are desired here. **"One should avoid the cruel day (Tuesday/Saturday), the night, the Empty days, the sixth lunar day, the twilight, and the birth constellation," according to Narada's words.**

In this context, he mentions the prohibition of shaving. Where is Tonsure heard of? **"One who has eaten, one who is fasting, one who has bathed, a king's servant, a young woman, one in early youth, a yogi, one facing travel or war... for these, the daily rites are destroyed if they shave."**
**"Parting the hair (Simantonnayana), fasting, wearing new clothes, traveling to a distant land, cutting the sacrificial wood, crossing the ocean—these should not be done if a shaving rite has been performed," so it is said.** Shaving and Tonsure are said to be the same. **"Having eaten" means one who has finished a meal. "Anointed" means one who has applied oil. "Fasting" is well-known. "King's servant" means a royal attendant.** Or "time." "Young woman" means a woman with her husband. "Early youth" means the first stage of life, **up to fifty years is the early age. "Yogi" means a Royal Yogi. "Facing travel or war" means one intent on a journey or a battle.** If the daily ritual is destroyed by the action of that day, these people should not be shaven. What is said in the subsequent verse regarding "physical work" should be connected here. Where shaving is a necessary cause, there they must be shaven. **Now, regarding the pregnant woman's husband,** following the prohibition of shaving, he states the non-prohibited exceptions. **"Above the parting..." etc. "Above the parting" means after the parting of the hair ceremony (Simantonnayana) has been performed for the wife. "Of the husband" means of the woman's husband.** Here it is said "except for necessary shaving." By this, it is understood that there is no fault in necessary shaving. Just as in the case of a wedding or traveling to another country, there is no fault in shaving. **For one who has established the sacred fires,** in the context of shaving, the shaving is done **month by month for the one who maintains the domestic fire or the fire-priest.** **Now he states the constellations for shaving and Tonsure. "Shaving and Tonsure are said to be the same," meaning what was said for Tonsure applies.**

At this point, in that specific lunar mansion <note>original: "Nakshatra"</note> and time, the ritual of **tonsure** <term>Kshaura: the ritual shaving of the head</term> is declared and prescribed. Now, regarding the rules for food consumption during the interval of the **tonsure ceremony** <term>Chaula: the first haircut of a child, usually in the third or fifth year</term>, as stated in the *Light of the Sun* by Gangadhara: In the absence of specific planetary afflictions, and when not at the Ganges or Vashishtha’s fire-altar—

The mother is named Sanjna and the father is Shravas; upon their actual death, the rule for a son without parents applies. In the matter of the rules for lunar mansions regarding tonsure, this is the secret. Here, in cases of separation and so on, there is no obstacle even for one whose father is living. For one whose father is living, there is no prohibition against tonsure.

In this very context, what is the story? It is said by **Brihaspati** <note>The divine preceptor and author of legal/astrological codes</note> regarding the consumption of food after a hundred vows or fasting: For those appointed to royal duties, for kings, and those serving the king, there is no fault in the division of the hair <note>tonsure</note> regardless of the specific time. According to the *Compendium of Astrology*: A royal practitioner, a city priest, and one whose parents are living—a husband whose wife is pregnant should not perform tonsure at any sacred pilgrimage sites. In another Law Code <note>original: "Smritantara"</note>: While bathing in the Indus, during the festival of the gods, or when traveling abroad, a husband whose wife is pregnant should not undergo tonsure. When there is a prohibition, one should perform it for the sake of the tonsure ritual; however, there is no fault in performing it during the funeral rites of a father. When the sun is in a particular division, or at the Ganges <gloss>or</gloss> when the sun is eclipsed, or when parents have died, or during the establishment of the sacred fire, or the drinking of Soma—in these six instances, tonsure is prescribed. One should not have a tonsure performed at a pilgrimage site if his wife is dressed in white <note>indicating pregnancy or specific ritual states</note>. Following the funeral procession and the **parting of the hair ritual** <term>Simantonnayana: a rite performed during pregnancy</term>, except for an occasional necessity, one should not perform tonsure before the fifth or eighth month; after that, one may undergo shaving according to one's wish.

Now begins the **commencement of learning the alphabet** <term>Akshararambha: the ritual beginning of a child's education</term>; its body of rules is described. With the Sun in the mansion of Mitra <note>Anuradha</note>, on the second or third day, with the "horses" <note>Ashvini</note>, or in the fifth—the deities are invoked. Before the **binding of the sacred thread** <term>Vratabandha: Upanayana or initiation</term>, first comes the beginning of learning. At the time to be described, the method of the letters <gloss>the alphabet</gloss> is given. Now, the beginning of the alphabet is auspicious, and so on is the essence. In which lunar mansion? In that which is devoid of Aquarius <note>Kumbha</note>, meaning not in an unfavorable sign. Regarding the characteristics of the solar half-year: in the six signs starting with Cancer, the **northern path** <term>Uttarayana: the period when the sun moves north, considered auspicious</term>, and in the southern, the gentle sacrifice, and so on. "Mitra" refers to Anuradha; "the hand" refers to the three stars of Hasta; "Punarvasu" <gloss>well</gloss>; "Indra" is Jyeshtha; "Shruti" is Shravana; "the horse" is Ashvini. With these lunar mansions, the acceptance of the alphabet is praised; this is the meaning. Before the thread ceremony, and after the tonsure <gloss>auspiciously</gloss> in the fifth year, the thread ceremony is prescribed. Therefore, after the tonsure <gloss>auspiciously</gloss>, if there is doubt about that fifth year, it is spoken of as being before the thread ceremony. Because of the statement of Brihaspati: "One should start the alphabet before the second birth <note>initiation</note>."

Anantasingha says: The acceptance of the alphabet is prescribed when the great five-year period is reached. When the sun is in its northern path, one should avoid the month of Aquarius. According to Vipramina: From Hasta onwards to the seventh sign, Mitra and Purujit; the ritual of the alphabet should be done on a lunar day that is pure, avoiding the first, the empty days <note>Rikta tithis</note>, the eighth, and the sixth, when the moon is in a favorable position, to avoid sorrow. <gloss>Now</gloss> Now the characteristics of the **conduct of vows** <term>Vratacharya</term>, both primary and secondary, at the time of the thread ceremony are given. For a Brahmin, in the eighth year from conception, when the Sun is favorable, or from birth, the vow should be performed. Even if the position is not ideal, one should always perform the commencement of the alphabet when Jupiter and the Sun are in the middle. They say it is best in the autumn, or in the fifth year. From conception—meaning from the time of the ritual for a male child—in the eighth year is the rule for the Brahmin; for the Kshatriya, it is in the eleventh; for the Vaishya, it is in the twelfth year. Now, when one's own year, Jupiter, and the Sun are auspicious, the vow should be performed. In the middle of the Sun...

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the proper timing for the Upanayana (sacred thread ceremony), specifically addressing the age limits for different social classes and the astrological considerations of Jupiter's position.</meta>

# Commentary on the Essence of the Auspicious (Su. Ma. Ti.)

For a **Brahmana** <note>The priestly or scholarly class</note>, the time extends from the eleventh year up to the twenty-second year; for a **Kshatriya** <note>The warrior or ruling class</note>, from the twelfth year up to the twenty-fourth year. || 5

---

<margin>Su. Ma. Ti. 24</margin> When the Sun is in Pisces <note>original: "Meena"</note> or Aries <note>original: "Mesha"</note>, and specifically during the month of Chaitra <note>March-April</note>, the vow <note>the Upanayana ceremony</note> should be performed. Why? Because it is fruit-bearing <note>effective</note>. This is stated in the *Sangraha* <note>A compendium of traditional laws</note>: "They say that in the year when the Sun is in Aries or Pisces during Chaitra, that Upanayana is auspicious." Even if the birth star is unfavorable, or if Jupiter is in Leo or in a debilitated state, the binding of the *Maunji* <note>the sacred girdle made of Munja grass</note> is declared auspicious when the Sun is in Aries or Pisces. Here, all planets moving in these signs become auspicious <unclear>...</unclear> just as time follows the stars. Now, he speaks of the secondary or extended time: "From one's own time, double the sequence," etc. This means from one's own primary age, the time is doubled. For a Brahmana, the eighth year is the primary time; doubled, it becomes the sixteenth year. From the eighth year to the sixteenth year, one may perform it in successive intervals. Now, he speaks of those who lose their right <note>become disqualified</note>. It is said in the *Sangraha*: "People who have passed the proper time are considered fallen." For one who has fallen from the *Savitri* <note>the Gayatri mantra/initiation</note>, purification must be sought among kinsmen. Thus, in the *Katyayana Grihya Sutra*: "The time for a Brahmana does not expire until the sixteenth year; for a Rajanya <note>Kshatriya</note> until the twenty-second; for a Vaishya <note>merchant/farmer class</note> until the twenty-fourth. Beyond this, they become fallen from the Savitri. One should not initiate them, nor teach them, nor perform sacrifices for them, nor have social dealings with them." If the time is exceeded, the offspring of those whose Savitri has fallen for two generations are neither to be initiated nor taught. Their purification is said to be through the *Vratyastoma* sacrifice <note>a ritual to reclaim those who have fallen out of the Vedic fold</note>, after which they may study if they wish. Elsewhere, regarding the time of Upanayana, it is said: "From the fifth or eighth year, or from the fifth, eighth, or ninth years, up to the eleventh or thirteenth, in sequence, according to the desire for Vedic study." The sages say these are the praised times. It is said in the *Sangraha*: "For one desiring spiritual luster, knowledge, long life, wealth, fame, and happiness, the initiation of a Brahmana and others should be performed from the fifth year for specific fruits." Now, to speak of the strength or weakness of Jupiter <note>Guru-bala</note>, he says: "From the sign..." If Jupiter is in the 1st, 3rd, 6th, 7th, 10th, or 11th house from the boy's birth sign, it is auspicious and brings victory. If it is in the 4th, 8th, or 12th, it is considered weak or harmful. <margin>24</margin> However, if Jupiter is in its own house <note>Sagittarius or Pisces</note> or in its sign of exaltation <note>Cancer</note>, then even if it is in these "bad" positions, it becomes beneficial. From the sign of Virgo onwards, the qualities are as stated. Even if the time has passed, if Jupiter is in a favorable house, the twice-born <note>initiated</note> person will find it auspicious. From the birth sign, if Jupiter is in the 6th house, it brings victory and is auspicious in the binding of the vow; otherwise, it is not. This is the connection. <margin>Rama</margin>

# Page 86

If the beloved <note>referring to a favorable planetary position</note> is equal, then by doubling the worship it becomes auspicious. Now the secrets: When Jupiter is in the signs of Pisces, Cancer, or Scorpio, or moving in even-numbered signs, it gives great results in marriages and initiations <term>Upanayana: the sacred thread ceremony for students</term>. In the birth chart, if Jupiter is in the <unclear>N</unclear> 4th or 8th house, it is auspicious through double worship. It is said that even if Jupiter is in a difficult position like the 12th, 4th, or 8th house, it becomes auspicious if it is not "pierced" <term>Vedha: an astrological obstruction caused by another planet</term> by other celestial bodies. If Jupiter is in the 2nd, 5th, 1st, 1st, or 9th positions, and is not obstructed by planets in the 12th, 4th, 8th, or 3rd positions, it gives good results. Specifically, Jupiter here is a giver of auspiciousness when not pierced by the 10 planets. The 2nd, 10th, 11th, 4th, 8th, 3rd, and 10th—in these numbers, if it is not pierced by a planet, then it is auspicious.
<gloss>Even if it is pierced in the shadow positions! Meaning: being obstructed by planets in the shadow numbers.</gloss>

<gloss>It would be excellently auspicious.</gloss> <gloss>In this context of the vow-binding <note>initiation</note> or marriage, if it is pierced, it will not be auspicious. For the sake of the pearl-like time <note>auspicious moment</note>, double binding etc. becomes auspicious. And so says Brihaspati: One should not consider the girl's Jupiter-strength after her first menses.</gloss>

Now, the same topic is considered regarding the doubling. Now he speaks of the months for the initiation ceremony. In the bright fortnight, in the five months starting with Madha <note>Chaitra</note>, on auspicious days of the Ashvin constellation, in the first half of the day. For the vow, avoid the days of non-study <term>Anadhyaya: days when Vedic study is prohibited</term>, the "empty" lunar days <term>Rikta Tithis: 4th, 9th, and 14th days of a lunar fortnight</term>, the 7th and 13th days, the Cancer portion of the moon, and the evening twilight. In the five months starting with Madha, in the bright fortnight, the Sun, Jupiter, and Venus are increasing in strength. On these days, in the first half:
<insert>The vow of initiation should be performed when these are good. Having mentioned the non-study days etc.</insert> The non-study days are the "empty" dates: the 4th, 9th, and 14th. The "Sage" is the 7th, "Madan" is the 13th. In the moon's portion, the Cancer division and the influence of malefic planets are forbidden first. The evening twilight is discussed in the section on non-study days.

Now he lists the constellations <term>Nakshatras</term> for the sacred thread ceremony. Excluding the "piercing" ones: Yamya, Agneya, Indra, and Pitru. Without these, the thread ceremony is auspicious. Jupiter, Venus, Mars, and the Moon are strong in their respective orders. If the Lord of the sign or the Moon is in its own division, and the Lord of the 4th house is a scholar of the Vedas, the householder becomes wealthy and a servant of the gods.
Yamya is Bharani. Agneya is Krittika. The "piercing" ones are Vishakha. Indra is Jyeshtha. Pitru is Magha. Excluding these five, the thread ceremony is auspicious in all others.
As it is said: "Magha, Bharani, Jyeshtha, Vishakha, and Krittika—if these five are obtained, the binding of the vow <note>initiation</note> should not be accepted." Thus says the Lord of the Gods.

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of Vratabandha (the sacred thread ceremony), specifically focusing on the planetary lords of the four Vedas and the astrological conditions for the initiate's future character and success. It references the "Muhurta Bhaskara Tika" (Commentary on the Sun of Auspicious Times).</meta>

<margin>
Commentary on Muhurta Bhaskara
Book of Gems
15
</margin>

When the lord [of the horoscope] is auspicious and endowed with strength of position and other factors, one should ask: who are the lords of the Vedas? He explains: Jupiter, Venus, Mars, and Mercury are respectively the lords of the Vedas. Jupiter is the lord of the *Rigveda*. Venus is the lord of the *Yajurveda*. Mars is the lord of the *Samaveda*. Mercury is the lord of the *Atharvaveda* <note>The text says "Mercury is the lord of the Yajurveda," but contextually implies the fourth Veda, the Atharvaveda, as Venus was already assigned the Yajurveda</note>. 

Now he describes the influence of the Ascendant <term>Lagna: the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the time of the ceremony</term>: The benefics, when situated in their own signs of exaltation or their own divisions, or even if the Lord of the Veda [associated with the boy] is in his own exaltation or division, or if they are placed in the quadrants <term>Kendra: the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses</term> or trines <term>Trikona: the 1st, 5th, and 9th houses</term>, the boy receiving the vow will become a knower of the meaning of the Vedas. Not only will he know the Vedas, but he will also be wealthy in his own home. 

However, if Saturn is in the quadrants or trines, the boy will become a servant of others or a lowly attendant. <insert>He will not merely be a knower of the Vedas, but also wealthy in his home.</insert> <note>The scribe appears to have repeated the previous positive line by mistake before correcting the sentiment</note>. Therefore, Saturn should not be in the quadrants or trines. It is said in the *Sangraha*: "If the Sun or Saturn are in the quadrants, the boy becomes a servant, performing the actions of a laborer, devoid of wealth and beauty, even if he is skilled in Vedic rituals."

Regarding the affliction of the Ascendant in this ceremony: If the Moon, the malefic Sun, the Lord of the Ascendant, and Venus are located in the sixth house, or if they are all in the eighth house <term>Randhra: the 8th house, associated with obstacles and death</term>, they become "six-fold fierce" or killers. In some cases, if the Sun is in the Ascendant, it is considered excellent. As stated in the *Maunji Patala*: "The Sun is the lord of all branches [of the Vedas]; therefore, in the thread ceremony of all people, the Sun is the primary lord." Some great sages say that in the absence of other strengths, the Sun in the Ascendant is praiseworthy. 

Some Munis <note>Sages</note> say that the Moon in the Ascendant is excellent if it is exalted. It is said: "The rising Moon is auspicious, but otherwise it causes disease. Especially in the Bright Fortnight <term>Shukla Paksha: the waxing phase of the moon</term>, it is beneficial." Now, regarding the Dark Fortnight <term>Krishnapaksha: the waning phase</term>: if the Moon rises in the Ascendant during the dark half, the initiate will be "falsely accused." "Falsely accused" means people in the world will wrongly say, "This person has committed a sin." He will also suffer from the "three diseases" <note>Likely referring to an imbalance of the three humors: Vata, Pitta, and Kapha</note>. Such are the effects of the Ascendant during the Dark Fortnight.

The verse beginning "In the Bright Fortnight..." seems to refer to the *Re-initiation* <term>Punah-Samskara: a second ceremony performed if the first was defiled or interrupted</term>. Since the effects of the Moon in the Ascendant for the Dark Fortnight were already stated, this must apply to specific cases. Re-initiation is permitted even at night to avoid the loss of religious merit, whereas the primary thread ceremony is strictly ordained for the morning/forenoon and never at night. The reasons for Re-initiation, such as "riding a donkey" and other acts, are well-known in the Dharma Shastras. Another text states: "The Sun in the 3rd, 11th, or 2nd houses is best; Saturn, Rahu, and Mars are good in the 3rd, 6th, and 11th. All benefics in the quadrants or trines are auspicious for the time of Vratabandha." Here, the third...

<summary>This page details the planetary rulers of the four Vedas and explains how the positions of planets like Jupiter, Venus, and Saturn at the time of the Upanayana ceremony determine the boy's future character and prosperity. It also distinguishes between the rules for the initial ceremony and the "Punah-Samskara" (re-initiation).</summary>
<keywords>Vratabandha, Upanayana, Vedic Astrology, Rigveda, Yajurveda, Samaveda, Atharvaveda, Punah-Samskara, Muhurta Bhaskara, Lagna, Saturn's influence</keywords>

--- Page 34 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of life-cycle rituals (Samskaras), specifically focusing on the timing for education and the martial initiation for royalty. It maintains the technical astrological tone of the previous sections.</meta>

<warning>Faded ink in some areas, noticeable staining and yellowing throughout the page. Modern digital watermark at the bottom center.</warning>

The possibility of a favorable outcome arises when the Ascendant <note>Lagna</note> is calculated for the exact time of night. Narada has spoken regarding the weighing of the Ascendant, and this is the authoritative statement. If the two malefic planets <note>Mars and Saturn</note> are situated in the Ascendant, the person born at that lunar day <note>Tithi</note> will constantly suffer from wasting diseases.

Just as the strength of the Ascendant is vital for a King, so it is for others. Now, the auspicious stars for the **Commencement of Education** <term>Vidyarambha: the ritual marking the start of formal schooling</term> are described. During the time joined with the sacred thread ceremony <note>Maunji</note>, one should begin education after worshiping the Goddess of Speech <note>Saraswati</note> according to the rites. This should be done when the moon is in fixed, movable, or dual signs, excluding the three "Uttara" constellations and Rohini. 

Education should be started at the time of the sacred thread ceremony. Having attained that time, one should begin the study of the Vedas. After performing the thread ceremony, one becomes authorized for study. What should be done? One should worship the Goddess of Speech, Saraswati, according to the prescribed path. How? At the time of the thread ceremony, in the fixed, movable, or dual signs, but avoiding the three Uttara stars and Rohini. These are to be avoided for the commencement of education. If education begins in a dual-natured sign, one attains great knowledge; in a fixed sign, there is dullness; in a movable sign, there is much wandering of the mind.

Now, regarding the **First Shaving** <term>Keshanta: the first ritual shaving of a young man's beard</term> and the **Removal of the Sacred Thread** <note>Maunji-vimochana</note>:
The Keshanta ritual should be performed like the tonsure ceremony <note>Choula</note> in the sixteenth year. Wise men say that for the removal of the sacred thread at the end of the vow, the strength of Jupiter <note>Guru-bala</note> need not be examined. For both Keshanta and the thread removal, the wise do not look for the strength of Jupiter. In all these matters, the benefactor's position is considered.

Now, for those who do not have the sacred thread ceremony, such as the warrior class, the **Binding of the Knife** <term>Kshurika-bandha: a martial initiation ceremony for princes involving the ritual tying of a sword or knife</term> is described. For the six classes of people who do not have the thread ceremony, the binding of the knife should be done on the lunar days and under the stars prescribed for the thread ceremony, but not when Jupiter or Venus are set <note>invisible in the sky</note>, and not on Tuesdays. It should be done in the months prescribed for marriage. 

As stated in the Compendium <note>Sangraha</note>: "For the six types of princes who lack the sacred thread, the binding of the knife should be performed on the lunar days prescribed for the thread ceremony, excluding Tuesday." 

Narada also says: "I shall describe the binding of the knife for Kings. It should be done in the months prescribed for marriage, in the bright fortnight, when the sun is in a northern transit. When Jupiter and Venus are strong and the Moon and stars are favorable, on the lunar days of the thread ceremony, excluding Tuesday. One should avoid the ninth division <note>Navamsha</note> of Aries and the rising of the sign Leo." 

When the Moon is in a favorable position, avoiding the 6th, 8th, and 12th houses from the Ascendant, with benefics in the 2nd, trines, or angles, and other planets in the 3rd, 6th, or 11th houses, the binding of the knife should be performed by those who know the rites. One should properly worship the knife in the presence of the deities. Then, at an auspicious moment, it should be bound. 

The omens of the ceremony are as follows:
- If the ceremony results in a "Flag" shape, he will be wealthy.
- If "Smoke," death.
- If "Lion," victory.
- If "Dog," freedom from disease.
- If "Bull," gain of wealth.
- If "Donkey," he will be sorrowful.
- If "Elephant," he will be happy and well-loved.
- If "Crow," loss of wealth.

# Commentary on the Muhurta Martanda
## On the Examination of Lineage and Qualities

<margin>Commentary on Muhurta Martanda</margin> Now, the author explains the consideration of qualities suitable for one's family through twelve verses. In this chapter, the knowledge of <unclear>Varna</unclear> (caste-class) is given, without which all efforts are in vain. Now begins the Chapter on Marriage. A groom should marry a bride endowed with good qualities, including her physical limbs, lineage, and family.

<margin>18</margin> ->The groom should wed a woman of good limbs and lineage. Here, the compatibility is determined by the sequence of **Varna** (class), **Vashya** (attraction), **Yoni** (nature), **Khechara** (planets/friendship), **Gana** (temperament), **Kuta** (agreement), and **Nadi** (pulse/constitution).<- These factors become progressively stronger in the order mentioned. In this system, the Brahmin and other classes are considered powerful in assembly.

<margin>16</margin> From here on, all verses are in the *Shardula Vikridita* <note>A complex Sanskrit poetic meter of 19 syllables</note> meter. Regarding "groom, lineage, and family," one should marry after considering the physical traits; without these, the union lacks various types of emotional depth. Lineage (*Gotra*) should be understood from the family tree. Family (*Kula*) should be examined up to the seventh and fifth generations. After knowing these two through the charts, one should look at the following.

Now, the author explains the sequence of the combination of points. **Varna** (1 point), **Vashya** (2 points), **Yoni** (4 points), **Khechara** (5 points), **Gana** (6 points), **Bhakoot** (7 points), and **Nadi** (8 points). In this order, the factors are increasingly powerful. Here, the auspiciousness of the match is determined by the total points. If the qualities exceed eighteen <note>out of 36</note>, the match is considered favorable. Here, he explains the strength:

Starting from Pisces, the classes such as Brahmin and others are known by repeated counting. Pisces is Brahmin; Aries is Kshatriya; Taurus is Vaishya; Gemini is Shudra. Then Cancer begins the cycle again <note>Cancer is Brahmin, Leo is Kshatriya, etc.</note>. In this world, the groom is auspicious if he is stronger than the bride; otherwise, the purpose is not achieved.

Now, he explains **Vashya** (mutual attraction/control) in one verse: ->Humans, edible creatures, water-dwellers, and forest-dwellers...<-
For a human, water-dwellers are "edible" (prey). For the same human, all others are "controllable" (*Vashya*), except for the Lion (Leo). This means all are controllable except the Lion. For the Lion (Leo), the Scorpion (Scorpio) is the one to be avoided. All signs except the Lion and Scorpion are controllable.

The Lion is an enemy to the human and not auspicious. Other relationships should be understood as "edible" and so on. For a human, the Goat and Lion are not compatible; therefore, a union between them should not be made. A Cow is controllable by a human; here, mutual friendship is seen. Therefore, there should be friendship between them. This should be considered even when observing the behavior of the bride and groom in the world. The forms of the zodiac signs should be understood from the *Brihat Jataka* <note>A foundational text by Varahamihira</note>: "The Fish (Pisces), the Water-bearer (Aquarius), the Twins (Gemini) with a lute, the Bull (Taurus)..." <margin>Ro 3 16</margin>

<margin>M 2</margin> "...the Bow (Sagittarius), the Sea-monster (Capricorn) with a deer's face, the Scales (Libra), and the Virgin (Virgo) in a boat with fire and grass. The remaining signs resemble their names." Thus say the wise.

Now, he explains the **Yoni** (animal type/sexual compatibility) in one verse: ->The Horse, Elephant, Sheep, Serpent, Dog, Cat, Rat, Cow, Buffalo, Tiger, Deer, Monkey, Mongoose, and Lion.<-
These are the animal types in order. One should count the constellations of the bride and groom. Count from the bride's constellation to the groom's, and from the groom's to the bride's. <margin>Rama</margin>

## Now, the Yonis (Animal Types) are described

Starting from Ashwini, the second and third constellations follow. From Ashwini onwards, the Yonis are as follows: The Horse and Elephant belong to Ashwini and Bharani. The Tiger belongs to Krittika. The Bull and Serpent belong to Rohini. The Serpent <note>specifically the King Cobra</note> belongs to Mrigashira. The Dog belongs to Ardra. The Cat <note>specifically the wild cat</note> belongs to Punarvasu <gloss>the skin of Punarvasu</gloss> <unclear>silent cat</unclear> is well known for Punarvasu and Pushya, and the Cat for Ashlesha. The Rat belongs to Magha and Purva Phalguni. The Cow belongs to Uttara Phalguni. The Buffalo belongs to Hasta. The Tiger belongs to Chitra. The Buffalo belongs to Swati. The Tiger belongs to Vishakha. The Deer belongs to Anuradha and Jyeshtha. The Dog belongs to Mula. The Monkey belongs to Purva Ashadha. The Mongoose belongs to Uttara Ashadha and Abhijit. The Monkey belongs to Shravana. The Lion belongs to Dhanishta. The Horse belongs to Shatabhisha. The Lion belongs to Purva Bhadrapada. The Cow belongs to Uttara Bhadrapada. The Elephant belongs to Revati. Thus ends the classification of Yonis...

## Now, the Hostility between Yonis is described

The Lion and Elephant, the Buffalo and Horse, the Cat and Rat, the Dog and Deer, the Monkey and Sheep, the Snake and Mongoose—these pairs are naturally fearful of each other and should be avoided for the bride and groom. The Lion and Elephant <note>original: "Simhe-ma"</note> are enemies. The Cat and Rat are enemies. The Dog and Deer, the Monkey and Sheep, the Cow and Tiger, the Mongoose and Snake are enemies. This is the "Vaira" (hostility). Between the bride and groom, if one is the servant and the other the master, this hostility must be avoided.

## Now, the Friendship and Hostility of the Planets

Now the lords of the zodiac signs are explained. Starting from Aries (Mesha), the qualities and lords are as follows: For the Sun, Jupiter, Mars, and the Moon are friends. Saturn and Venus are enemies. Mercury is neutral. For the Moon, there are no enemies; the Sun and Mercury are friends. All others are neutral. For Mars, the Sun, Moon, and Jupiter are friends; Mercury is the enemy. Others are neutral. For Mercury, the Sun and Venus are friends; the Moon is the enemy. Others are neutral. For Jupiter, Venus and Mercury are enemies; the Sun, Mars, and the Moon are friends. Others are neutral. For Venus, the Sun and Moon are enemies; Saturn and Mercury are friends. For Saturn, Mars, the Moon, and the Sun are enemies; Venus and Mercury are friends. This is the established opinion regarding the friendship and hostility of the lords of the signs. || 5 ||

Starting from Aries (Mesha), the lords of the signs are as follows: Mars is the lord of Aries. Venus is the lord of Taurus, and so on, as is clear. For the Sun (Surya), Jupiter (Guru), Mars (Mangala), and the Moon (Chandra) are friends. Saturn (Shani) and Venus (Shukra) are enemies. For the Moon (Chandra), there is no enemy; the Sun and Mercury are friends. All others are neutral. For Mars (Mangala), Mercury is the enemy; the Sun, Moon, and Jupiter are friends. Others are neutral. For Mercury (Budha), Venus and the Sun are friends; the Moon is the enemy. Others are neutral. For Jupiter (Guru), Venus and Mercury are enemies; the Sun, Mars, and Moon are friends. Others are neutral. For Venus (Bhrigu), the Sun and Moon are enemies; Saturn and Mercury are friends.

4. Different signs, same star quarter.

# Section 5

Therefore, he does not speak of a specific lion <note>original: "simha-vishesha"; likely referring to the Leo zodiac sign or a specific strength calculation</note> here or there. Regarding the woman, it is said: a lion is indeed formidable. When joined with a woman or maiden, it is feminine. Now, he explains the *Nadi* <term>Nadi: one of the eight primary factors in marriage compatibility, representing the physiological/spiritual temperament</term>.

### On the Pulse (Nadi)

In the root <note>Mula constellation</note>, the Sun, the star of the Sun, and the noose-bearer... <note>The text here lists constellations associated with the three Nadis: Adi (Initial), Madhya (Middle), and Antya (Final)</note>.

The verse indicates the three *Nadis* through three feet of the stanza.
1. **First Nadi:** Mula, Jyeshtha (Indra), Shatabhisha (the noose-bearer), Purva Bhadrapada (the one-footed goat), Punarvasu (Aditi), Uttara Phalguni (Aryaman), Ahirbudhnya (the lord), and Ashwini. These nine stars constitute one *Nadi*.
2. **Second Nadi:** Bharani (Yama), Mrigashira (the Moon), Pushya (the teacher/Jupiter), Anuradha (the friend), Purva Phalguni (fortune), Dhanishta (the Vasus), Chitra (Tvastar), and Uttara Bhadrapada. These nine stars constitute the second *Nadi*.
3. **Third Nadi:** The remaining nine stars constitute the third *Nadi*.

Thus, there are three *Nadis*. In the context of marriage, when the bride and groom share the same *Nadi* <note>Nadi Dosha</note> within these groups of nine, it results in the death of the couple.

### Regional and Caste Exceptions

Now, according to the venerable Gautama, there is a specific rule for the regions of the Kshatriyas <note>the warrior/ruling class</note> and others. He explains the course of action when a match is not otherwise found. In the Southern Country <note>Dakshina Desha</note>, for all castes and for those who are leaders, if another suitable maiden is not available, the marriage <note>grasping of the hand</note> is considered beneficial and auspicious even if there are Nadi complications. However, death is predicted specifically in the "Middle *Nadi*."

As stated in the *Ratnakosha* <note>The "Jewel-Mine," a famous astrological treatise</note>:
> If the "Front *Nadi*" is afflicted, the husband dies; if the "Middle *Nadi*" is afflicted, the bride dies; if the "Back *Nadi*" is afflicted, the maiden dies—of this there is no doubt. In a "close piercing" <note>vedha</note>, death comes quickly; in a "distant piercing," it occurs after a long time.

<insert>By the measurement of the interval... if it arises. This pertains to the Brahmins... <unclear>...</unclear> <unclear>as explained in the Nirukta...</unclear></insert>

These two are merely paths, and therefore one should avoid them. Thus, in the *Jyotish Prakasha* <note>Light of Astrology</note> regarding the Kshatriya class: in the event of the Middle *Nadi*, the couple should not marry. The arrangement for this is explained therein. The saying that "a single *Nadi* is not forbidden in marriage" refers specifically to the Kshatriyas and other classes, according to the opinion of Gautama.

Now, regarding the auspiciousness of the previously mentioned "distant piercing" of the *Nadis*: if the planets are distant and auspicious, then it is favorable.

When the woman's star and the man's star are analyzed, if they are external to the "Vashya" <note>attraction/control</note> faces, and joined with the strengths of the Kuta and Nadi, it shall be auspicious. Now he speaks of the "distant communication." From the woman's star, if the man's star is near, then it shall be considered "distant"...

# Now, the results of the Moon-sign compatibility (Bhakuta) are stated.

That <term>Bhakuta</term><note>The compatibility of the zodiac signs of the bride and groom.</note> defect is neutralized if the lords of the signs are mutual friends, even if the distance between the signs is unfavorable. "Udu-maitri" refers to the friendship of the birth stars (<term>Nakshatras</term>). So too is the friendship of the "Yoni" <note>The animal type associated with the birth star.</note>. Now, the gifts to be given to alleviate the distress of unfavorable compatibility are stated: For the 2nd and 12th positions, give copper or gold; for the 6th and 8th, give a pair of cows; for the 5th and 9th, give silver to a learned person for the sake of prosperity. If the Sun is in the 2nd or 12th, gold should be given. If in the 6th or 8th, a pair of cows; if in the 5th or 9th, silver should be given to a learned person for the sake of prosperity.

## Now, the gift for the single-pulse (Eka-Nadi) defect is stated.
If there is a <term>Nadi</term><note>The "pulse" or physiological temperament in Vedic astrology; having the same Nadi is considered a major defect for a couple.</note> defect, give a cow, gold, and clothing to a wise man for the sake of peace.

<margin>As stated in the Rajamartanda <note>A classical Sanskrit text on Muhurta (electional astrology) attributed to King Bhoja.</note></margin>: In the 6th and 8th positions, give a pair of cows; in the 5th and 9th, silver for prosperity. If there is a Nadi defect, gold must be given. In the 2nd, 6th, 10th, etc., these are the results. It is said: for the 2nd and 12th, give gold; for the 5th and 9th, silver; for the 6th and 8th, a cow and a feast for Brahmins; and for the Nadi defect, gold.

# Now, the method for considering the points (Gunas) is described.

<margin>First, the Varna (class) is stated.</margin>
<margin>Analysis 5</margin>
If the <term>Varna</term><note>The spiritual/social grade based on the zodiac sign.</note> is the same or the groom's is higher, 1 point is known. In <term>Vashya</term><note>Mutual attraction or control between signs.</note>, if there is mutual control, 2 points; if they are mutual friends, 2 points; if one is food for the other <note>e.g., a lion and a deer</note>, 0 points; if they are natural enemies, 0 points; if one is the enemy of the other's controller, 0 points. If they are neutral, 1 point. If they are friends, 2 points. If one is a friend and the other an enemy, 1 point. If they are great enemies, 0 points. If both are mutual friends, 5 points. If they are neutral-friends, 4 points. If they are neutral-neutral, 3 points. If they are neutral-enemy, 2 points. If they are enemy-enemy, 1 point. For <term>Gana</term><note>Temperament: Divine, Human, or Demonic.</note>, if both are the same, 6 points. If one is Human and the other Divine, 5 points. If one is Human and the other Demonic, 0 points.

If the <term>Bhakuta</term> is bad but the <term>Yoni</term> friendship is strong, the defect is removed. If the <term>Yoni</term> is the same, 4 points; if they are friends, 3 points; if neutral, 2 points; if enemies, 1 point; if great enemies, 0 points. If the <term>Nadi</term> is different, 8 points.

Regarding "Bhu" (1 point): If the Varna is the same or the groom's is higher, one point is understood. This means if the groom belongs to a higher Varna than the bride, or if they are the same, they both receive one point. However, if the groom is of a lower Varna, there are no points. As stated in the *Jyotirnibandha*: "One point is present if the man is of a higher Varna. If the man is of a lower Varna, the wise say the points are destroyed; if equal, half a point." <note>The text here notes a variation in traditional opinions regarding equal Varnas.</note>

# Now, the consideration of Vashya (Mutual Control) points is stated.
In Vashya, 2 points are given. If it is half-Vashya, 1 point. For example, for humans, water-dwellers (like fish) are "Vashya" (controllable); there, half a point is taken. This applies to Brahmins and others. Sometimes, among the twice-born <note>High-caste Hindus</note>, there are those devoted to their specific lineage, but not all. Therefore, the consideration of lower castes should be applied. As said in the verse "Zero for enemies," the lion is the controller of all. Among them, humans are enemies, and fish and elephants are also enemies. Others are controllable. If they are mutual friends, 2 points should be known. From an analytical perspective, humans have friendship with sheep, bulls, horses, and deer. There, if they are of lower species, they are controllable and receive half a point. Elsewhere, 2 points.

# On the Compatibility of Signs and Stars

**The Moon-sign lord is one point.** <note>original: "Rakedhara" referring to the lord of the sign</note> Here is the specific detail: Between humans, the Scorpion and the Lion <note>Scorpio and Leo</note> are in a state of natural enmity at night. It is well known in the world that the Scorpion kills the Lion, or the Lion meets death through the Scorpion. Therefore, in this case, there is a lack of compatibility points <term>Guna-abhava: the absence of the numerical points assigned in matchmaking</term>.

First, one should consider the rising sign <term>Lagna</term> at birth and other factors; therefore, the wise must deliberate carefully. This thought has occurred to people regarding the consideration of the bride and groom. What has been said here by the learned should be refined. Now, you might ask: "If it is said that everything is under the power of the Lord of the fruit of the Lagna, why did you say that humans are like wild enemies?" It is true; however, the Lagna is general to everyone, whereas here the author of the book refers to the specific birth details. The Lion, being of a noble and majestic nature, may face death, but it is indifferent to killing others because they are not its equal. It is said: "In the absence of enmity, there is a full presence of points; in friendship, two points; in cases of subjugation or enmity, one point; and in cases of sign-division, one point."

### Now, the consideration of the Star points (Tara Guna) is explained:
When there is friendship and both stars are auspicious, there are three points. This means: if by the method of calculation both stars are found to be auspicious, then three points are awarded. If they are "mixed"—meaning the star is auspicious on one side but inauspicious on the other—then it is a "fraction" <note>original: "shakala"</note>. That fraction of the three points results in one and a half points. Otherwise, a total lack of points is established. As it is said: "If one side receives an auspicious star and the other does not, then one and a half points are given. If neither has an auspicious star, then one should declare zero points."

<insert>Now, the consideration of the Animal Type points (Yoni Guna) is explained:</insert>
For great friends, there are four points <note>original: "pratara guna" likely referring to the highest grade</note>. For friends, three. For those who are neutral or natural opposites, one. For natural qualities, two. For great enemies, there is very little or no point awarded. Thus, there are five types of Yoni points. 

**The classification is as follows:**
Among the twenty-eight constellations <term>Nakshatras</term> starting with Ashwini, there are fourteen animal types <term>Yonis</term>. Among them, the Lion and the Elephant, or the Buffalo and the Tiger, etc., are said to be "Great Enemies." Even among Great Enemies, the Buffalo, Tiger, Monkey, and Ram—these pairs are considered to have a neutral natural quality <unclear>due to the absence of a striker</unclear>.

Now, among those of the same animal type, great friendship should be understood. However, even among the same types, such as the Dog and the Cat, or the Tiger and the Lion, even if they share a category, they are not friends because they fight with one another; these five pairs have only "natural" points. In these five pairs, there is the highest degree of "Great Enmity." The Buffalo, Tiger, Monkey, and Ram—these seven pairs have natural quality points. The Tiger is neutral and favorable to all <note>interpretive note: this may refer to a specific astrological hierarchy where the Tiger does not initiate petty quarrels</note>. However, the Tiger and Lion are enemies to others. There is enmity between the Snake and the Cat. Similarly, there is friendship between the Cow and the Buffalo. There is enmity between the Rat and the Snake. Thus, having considered enemies, friends, and neutrals, the points should be understood as stated. 

Regarding the acceptance of neutral natural points: although the points for neutrals are not explicitly stated, one sees neutrality in the Buffalo, Horse, Ram, and Monkey. Therefore, in these neutral cases, the points must be calculated according to the tradition. It is said: "In enmity, natural state, friendship, and great friendship, the points are 0, 2, 3, and 4 respectively." <gloss>These are the points.</gloss> 

### Now, the consideration of the Planetary points (Khechara Guna)...

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of astrological compatibility (Kuta) for marriage, specifically focusing on planetary friendship, temperament, and the auspicious age for a bride's marriage. It follows the previous page's focus on matching horoscopes.</meta>

<margin>18</margin> He says: "One..." <note>referring to planetary friendship</note> Where there is one lord <gloss>new</gloss>, that is one. Where both are friends, that is "both-friends." Where one is an enemy and both are friends, that is "one-enemy-both-friends." For these, five points <term>guna: units of merit in compatibility</term> should be taken.
<margin>19</margin> The meaning is that in both cases, five points should be taken. Now, if two are equal enemies, or one is equal and one is neutral: "Arati" means enemy. Thus, in a match where there is an equal enemy, three points are for one half. Similarly, in "equal-friend," there should be four points. In "enemy-benefic" or "enemy-friend" situations, there is one point. In "neutral," there are three. Where both are neutral, three points should be taken. Where not specified, there is an absence of points. It is also said: where the lords of both signs are friends, five points; four if they are equal friends; if both are equal, three points; if one is a friend and the other an enemy, one point; if one is equal and the other an enemy, one point. If there is mutual intense enmity, zero points are prescribed.

### Now he discusses the consideration of Temperament (Gana) points.

He says: Where the groom is of the Divine temperament <term>Deva Gana</term> and the bride is of the Human temperament <term>Manushya Gana</term>, here six points should be taken. In the reverse of this, five points <note>original: "shara-mita," measured by arrows, a poetic term for five</note> should be taken. This is what is said: where the groom is Human and the bride is Divine, five points are to be taken. If the temperaments are the same, six points should be taken. Or rather, if the man is of his own temperament and the bride is of the Divine temperament, one point is taken. Elsewhere it is said that in other cases, six or other points should be taken.

### Now he discusses the consideration of the Sign-Distance (Bhakuta) points.

In the "Bad-Bhakuta" <note>unfavorable relative positions of the moon signs</note>, such as the 2nd/12th or 5th/9th positions, if the sexual compatibility <term>Yoni Maitri</term> is strong, then the points are clear <note>meaning the defect is cancelled</note>. Here, if one of the two is strong in sexual compatibility, then one point is given. If they share the same constellation <term>Nakshatra</term>, the points are clear. In this "Bhakuta," if the groom and bride have sexual enmity, then six points should be taken <note>this likely refers to specific cancellation rules</note>. Where they are in friendly signs, or the same sign, or different signs with the same lord, five points are taken. In other remaining cases, seven points <note>original: "munaya," the seven sages</note> are taken.

### Now he discusses the consideration of the Pulse (Nadi) points.

Where the "Pulses" <term>Nadi</term> are different, eight points <note>original: "gaja," elephants, meaning eight</note> should be taken. Where not specified, there is an absence of points. Thus it is settled.

### Now he discusses the time for a girl's marriage.

From the eighth year of a woman's life, through the third year following, in the even years of the cycles, marriage is auspicious. One should avoid the eldest son marrying the eldest daughter <note>the "Jyestha" restriction</note>. One should avoid marriage in the birth month or under the birth star for the first-born child.
1. From the eighth year of a woman, in every third year, marriage is auspicious. Here, marriage in the eighth, ninth, or tenth years is auspicious. Thus says Samvarta: <margin>19</margin>

> <margin>Rama:</margin> An eight-year-old girl is "Gauri"; a nine-year-old is "Rohini."
> A ten-year-old is a "Kanya" <term>maiden</term>; after twelve, she is "Rajasvala" <term>pubescent</term>.

By giving a Gauri in marriage, one attains the world of the Serpents and Vaikuntha <note>Heaven of Vishnu</note>; by giving a Rohini, the world of Brahma; by giving a Rajasvala, one goes to the Raurava hell. "Rajasvala" is the term used. Thus it is in the Karika:

> Girls should be married within the six-year period; after two years,
> Soma <note>the Moon</note> enjoys her, then the Gandharva <note>celestial musician</note>, and then Agni <note>Fire</note>.

Thus... <note>Catchword: "ityapa" - leading to the next page</note>

# On the Timing of Marriage

The Vedic scripture <note>Shruti</note> regarding the "garland of one's own choice" <note>Svayamvara</note> begins with the sequence: "Soma was the first possessor, then the Gandharva..." and "Soma gave her to the Gandharva, and the Gandharva gave her to Agni." <note>This refers to the Rigvedic hymn 10.85, suggesting a girl is protected by deities before her human marriage.</note> Regarding a contradiction to this, the sage **Brihaspati** spoke in the Puranas, following the greatness of Gautama, concerning the claim that "Soma gave her to a human."

**Vishnu** said: "After the fourth year and until the tenth festival <note>the tenth year</note>, the father must make every effort to perform the marriage of the daughter."

When such contradictions arise, the rule of logic states that the Puranas and Smritis <note>remembered tradition</note> are superior in some cases, while the Vedas <note>heard revelation</note> are superior in others. Thus, the Puranic statement refers to the Shudra class and others, while the Vedic and Smriti rules refer to Brahmins and others; in this way, there is no contradiction.

For women in their seventh year, <gloss>yellow highlight</gloss> **Narada** and others have specified the timing. Therefore, the teacher Gana, who knows the essence of the Vedas, has stated what is not contradictory for all classes. Some say that according to certain texts, after the eighth year of women, if a suitable groom is found, marriage may occur in even-numbered years such as the eighth, tenth, fourteenth, or sixteenth, as stated in other fields of law.

In the commentary on the <gloss>yellow highlight</gloss> **Kalanirnayadipika** <note>The Lamp of Determining Time</note>, two periods are mentioned for choosing one's own husband <note>Svayamvara</note>:
1. For a girl like Jivanti who has not reached ten years, the time for choosing a husband is two years after she reaches puberty.
2. Even if she has not reached puberty, the time for choosing a husband is after three years <note>of waiting for the father to arrange it</note>.

By this, the requirements for the "giving of a daughter" <term>Kanyadana - the ritual gift of a bride</term> common to all classes are stated in the <gloss>yellow highlight</gloss> **Kalanirnayadipika**: "If the father, grandfather, brother, mother, or kinsman do not give her away, the girl may choose her own husband after three years from the onset of puberty."

<gloss>yellow highlight</gloss> **Baudhayana** also says: "A girl should wait three years for her father's command after reaching puberty; in the fourth year, she should find a husband of equal status."

### The Timing for Men
Now he states the time for a man's marriage: One should not wait until the very end of the religious vows. Marriage is auspicious in even-numbered years, not odd-numbered years. Regarding specific seasons, he says: "In the pairs of Radha and Magha." Radha is the month of Vaishakha <note>April-May</note>. Magha is well-known <note>January-February</note>. In these pairs—Vaishakha and Jyeshtha, or Magha and Phalguna—and also in Margashirsha <note>November-December</note>, marriage is auspicious. These five months are highly approved.

Regarding the month of Jyeshtha <note>May-June</note>: If both the bride and groom are the first-born <note>Jyeshtha</note> of their respective families, one should avoid this month. However, if only one is the first-born, <gloss>yellow highlight</gloss> **Narada** says it need not be avoided. Another authority says: "One should not perform the marriage of two first-borns in the month of Jyeshtha, nor in the month of Phalguna."

Regarding planetary positions: One should avoid the time when the Moon is in the first sign, or when Mercury and Jupiter are associated with the house of the Sun, or when associated with malefic planets, as these yield undesirable results. It is said in the *Vivaha-Vrindavana*: "Even a friend among the auspicious stars will not save one <note>from a bad omen</note>." This flaw is common to all auspicious deeds.

### Regarding Birth Months
Now, regarding the section on what to avoid, he gives a specific decision on birth months: "One should avoid the month of one's birth for the sake of the first pregnancy's success." Here, <gloss>yellow highlight</gloss> **Narada** says: "The marriage of the first-born son or daughter should not take place in their birth month, under their birth star, or on their birthday."

# Muhurta Martanda Commentary, Page 20

He now describes the obstructions regarding the marriage stars. **"Mula, Uttara, etc."** Mula is well-known. "Uttara" refers to Revati <note>The commentator clarifies specific star groupings here</note>. "Prako" refers to Hasta. Magha is well-known. "Fixed" refers to the fixed stars: Rohini and the three Uttaras (Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada). "Durmukha" refers to Swati. Along with these, Maitra (Anuradha) is a beneficial star for marriage.

Now he explains the specific details of **Vedha** <term>Vedha: An "obstruction" or harmful alignment between two stars or planets that renders a time inauspicious</term>. A star associated with a malefic planet, or one recently released from an eclipse, or one that has been "pierced," is not auspicious. This means: a star occupied by a malefic planet and devoid of the Moon is not auspicious. It is stated in the *Samhita Pradipaka*: "A star released by Rahu <note>The North Node of the Moon, associated with eclipses</note> remains inauspicious for three months; one released by Saturn for four months; and one released by Mars or Venus for two months." "Associated with the wicked" means joined with malefic planets.

Now the obstructions are listed:
*   Between Mrigashira and Uttara Ashadha.
*   Between Bharani and Anuradha.
*   Between Rohini and Abhijit.
*   Between Magha and Shravana.

There is a mutual obstruction between these pairs. Also between Revati and Uttara Phalguni; Punarvasu and <unclear>Ashravavarajnasah</unclear>—which means Mula. Between Mula and Punarvasu; between Shatabhisha and Swati; and between the two Hastas <note>likely referring to Hasta and its counterpart in the grid</note>, there is mutual obstruction. This connects to the following verse.

Now he explains the decision regarding Vedha in specific rituals. **"Obstruction in the 'Grasping of the Hand' (Marriage)..."** This has a three-fold explanation. The obstruction mentioned for the "Grasping of the Hand" (Marriage) is declared by all the Sages. In other rituals, it is not considered, except in those involving the **Sapta-shalaka-chakra** <term>Sapta-shalaka-chakra: A 'seven-slat' grid used by astrologers to calculate planetary obstructions</term>. 

<insert>In what kind of Sapta-shalaka?</insert> 

Starting from the direction of Shiva (Northeast), combined with Krittika and the others. "Karsinava" refers to the deity Agni, thus meaning the stars starting from Krittika, arranged clockwise around the directions. Except for marriage, in all other rituals, the obstruction should be observed using this Seven-Slat Grid. 

In the grid known as Sapta-shalaka, obstruction in all rituals must be avoided, especially in marriage, as is the case with the "Five-Line" grid.

Now he describes the obstruction of the "quarters" (Padas) <note>Each star is divided into four quarters</note>. There is mutual obstruction between the 4th and 1st quarters, and between the 2nd and 3rd quarters. He gives the decision on this: "In the case of a 'piercing' by a benefic planet, one should only avoid that specific quarter." As stated by the sage Mandavya: "If a star is pierced by benefic planets, avoid only that quarter. But if pierced by cruel (malefic) planets, the entire star must be abandoned." This is the certainty regarding Vedha. Here, "pierced by a benefic, only a quarter; pierced by a wicked one, the whole."

Now he gives the exceptions to the obstruction rule. **"When the Lord of the Ascendant is in the House of Gains..."** 
1. If the Lord of the Ascendant is in the 11th House, there is no fault from the obstruction. (First cancellation).
2. Or, if the Moon is aspected by a friend. (Second cancellation).
3. Or, if the Ascendant is occupied by a planet other than the Moon. (Third exception).
4. If the Lord of the Ascendant is a benefic. (Fourth exception).

If even one of these conditions is met, all authorities declare there is no fear of obstruction. 

**Vaidyanatha** says: "Avoid the beginning and end of the two..." and **Vasishtha** says: "Even with malefic planets, avoid the quarter... If the Lord of the Ascendant is joined with a benefic, or if the Lord of the Ascendant is in the House of Gains, or if the hour belongs to a benefic, it does not lead to a fault."
